10.3.3  Dynamic  parameter  2820 
Parameters accessible through SDCI read or write services may also  be changed via on-board  2821 
control elements (for example teach -in button) or the human machine interface of a Device . 2822 
These changes shall undergo the same validity checks as a single parameter access. Thus, in 2823 
case of a positive result "DataValid" in Figure 86, the "StoreRequest" flag shall be applied in 2824 
order to achieve Data Storage consistency.  In case of a negative result "InvalidData", the 2825 
previous values of the corresponding parameters shall be restored ("roll back"). In addition, a 2826 
Device  specific indication on the human machine interface is recommen ded as a positive or 2827 
negative feedback to the user.  2828 

===PAGE 162===
V1.1.5  – 162 – Interface and System  © IO -Link  
It is recommended to avoid concurrent access to a parameter via local control elements and 2829 
SDCI write services at the same point in time.  2830

10.3.5  Block Parameter  2846 
User applications such as function blocks within PLCs and parameterization tool software can 2847 
use start and end commands to indicate the begin and end of a Block Parameter  transmission. 2848 
For the duration of the Block Parameter transmission the Device application shall inhibit all 2849 
changes to Read/Write parameters originating from other sources, for example local 2850 
parameterization, teach -in, etc.  In case parameter access is locked, any user application shall  2851 
unlock “Parameter (write) access” (see Table B. 12) prior to downloading a parameter set.  2852

10.3.6  Concurrent p arameter ization  access  2879 
There is no mechanism to secure parameter consistency within the Device  in case of concurrent 2880 
accesses from different user applications above Master  level. This shall be ensured or blocked  2881 
on user level  (see 13.2.2 ). 2882

10.4  Data Storage (DS)  2889 
10.4.1  General  2890 
The Data Storage (DS) mechanism enables  the consistent and up -to-date buffering of the 2891 
Device  parameters on upper levels like PLC programs or fieldbus parameter server. Data 2892 
Storage between Master s and Device s is specified within this standard , whereas the adjacent 2893 
upper data storage mechanisms depend on the individual fieldbus or system . The Device  holds  2894 
a standardized set of objects providing  information about  parameters for Data Storage  such as  2895 
memory size requirements  as well as  control and state information of the Data Storage 2896 
mechanism  (see Table B. 10). Revisions of Data Storage parameter sets are identified via a  2897 
Parameter C hecksum.  2898 

===PAGE 167===
Interface and System  © IO -Link  – 167 – V1.1.5  
During Data Storage the Device shall apply the same checking rules as specified for the Block 2899 
Parameter  transfer in 10.3.5 . 2900 
The implementation of the DS mechanism specified  in this standard  is highly recom mended for 2901 
Devices. If this mechanism is not supported,  it is the responsibility of the Device vendor to 2902 
describe how pa rameterization of a Device after replacement can be ensured in a system 2903 
conform manner without tools.  2904 
10.4.2  Data Storage  state machine  2905 
Any changed set of valid parameters leads to a new Data Storage upload.  The upload is initiated 2906 
by the Device  by raising a "DS_UPLOAD_REQ " Event  (see Table D. 1). The Device  shall store 2907 
the internal state "Data Storage Upload " in non -volatile memory (see Table B. 10, State 2908 
Property ), until  it receives a Data Storage command "DS_UploadEnd " or "DS_DownloadEnd" . 2909 
The Device  shall generate an Event  "DS_UPLOAD_REQ " (see Table D. 1) only if the parameter 2910 
set is valid and  2911 
• parameters  assigned  for Data Storage  have been changed locally on the Device  (for 2912 
example teach -in, human machine interface, etc.) , or  2913 
• parameters assigned for Data Storage have been changed by the SystemCommand 2914 
Application Reset, or  2915 
• the Device  receives a SystemCommand  "ParamDownloadStore " 2916 
With this Event  information the Data Storage mechanism of  the Master  is triggered and initiates 2917 
a Data Storage  upload  or download sequence  depending on port configuration . The state 2918 
machine in Figure 90 specifies  the Device  Data Storage mechanism.  2919 
 2920 
Figure 90 – The Data Storage (DS) state machine  2921 
Table 100 shows the state transition tables of the Device  Data Storage (DS) state machine.  2922 
See Table B. 10 for details on DataStorageIndex assignments.  2923

10.4.3  DS configuration  2932 
The Data Storage mechanism inside the Device may be disabled via the Master, for example 2933 
by a tool or a PLC program . See B.2.4  for further details.  This is recommended during 2934 
commissioning or system tests to avoid intensive communication.  2935 
NOTE    This functionality will be removed in future releases and the Data  Storage mechanism will then  only be  2936 
 controlled via port configuration in the master . 2937 
10.4.4  DS memory space  2938 
To handle the requested data amount for Data Storage under any circumstance s, the requested 2939 
amount of indices to be saved  and the required total memory space  are given in the Data 2940 
Storage Size parameter, see Table B. 10. The required total memory space (including the 2941 
structural information  shall not exceed 2 048 octets  (see Annex  G). The Data Storage 2942 
mechanism  of the Master  shall be able to support this amount of memory per port . 2943 
10.4.5  DS Index _List  2944 
The Device is the "owner" of the DS Index_List  (see Table B. 10). Its purpose is to provide all 2945 
the necessary information for a Device replacement. The DS Index_List shall be fixed for any 2946 
specific DeviceID . Otherwise the data integrity between Master  and Device  cannot be 2947 
guaranteed.  The Index List shall contain the termination marker ( see Table B. 10), if the Device 2948 
does not support Data Storage (see 10.4.1 ). The required storage size shall be 0 in this case.  2949 
10.4.6  DS parameter availability  2950 
All indices listed in the Index List shall be readable and writeable between the 2951 
SystemCommand s "DS_UploadStart" or "DS_DownloadStart" and "DS_UploadEnd" or 2952 
"DS_DownloadEnd" ( see Table B. 10). If one of the Indices is rejected by the Device , the Data 2953

10.4.7  DS without ISDU  2956 
The support of ISDU  transmission in a Device  is a precondition for the Data Storage of 2957 
parameters. Parameters in Direct Parameter page 2 cannot be saved and restored  by the Data 2958 
Storage mechanism.  2959 
10.4.8  DS parameter change indication  2960 
The Parameter_ Checksum specified  in Table B. 10 is used as an indicator for changes in a 2961 
parameter set. This standard does not require a specific mechanism for detecting parameter 2962 
changes. A set of recommended methods is provided in the informative Annex  K. 2963 
10.5  Event Dispatcher  (ED) 2964 
Any of the Device  applications can generate predefined system status information when SDCI 2965 
operations fail or technology specific information (diagnosis) as a result from technology 2966 
specific diagnostic methods  occur . The Event Dispatcher turns this information into an Event  2967 
according to the definitions in A.6. The Event consists of an EventQualifier indicating the 2968 
properties of an incident and an EventCode ID representing a description of this incident 2969 
together with possible remedial measures. Table D. 1 comprises a list of predefined IDs and 2970 
descriptions for application -oriented  incidents. Range s of IDs are reserved for profile specific 2971 
and vendor  specific incidents. Table D. 2 comprises a list of predefined IDs for SDCI specific 2972 
incidents.  2973 
Events are classified in "Errors", "Warnings", and "Notifications". See 10.10.2  for these 2974 
classifications and see 11.6  for how the Master  is controlling and processing these Event s. 2975 
All Event s provided at one  point in time are acknowledged with one single command. Therefore,  2976 
the Event  acknowledgment  may be delayed by the slowest acknowledgment  from upper  system 2977 
levels . 2978 
10.6  Device f eatures  2979 
10.6.1  General  2980 
The following Device  features  are defined to a certain degree in order to achieve a common 2981 
behavior. They are accessible via standardized or Device  specific methods  or parameters.  The 2982 
availability of these features is defined in the IODD of a Device.  2983 
10.6.2  Device backward compatibility  2984 
This feature enables a Device  to play the role of a previous Device  revision. In the start -up 2985 
phase t he Master  System Management  over writes the Device 's inherent Device ID (DID) with 2986 
the requested former DeviceID. The Device 's technology application shall switch to the former  2987 
functional sets or subsets assigned to this  DeviceID . Device backward compatibility support is 2988 
optional for a Device . 2989 
As a Device can provide backward compatibility to previous DeviceIDs (DID), these compatible 2990 
Devices shall support all parameters and communication capa bilities of the previous Device ID. 2991 
Thus, the Device is permitted to change any communication  or identification  parameter in this 2992 
case.   2993 
10.6.3  Protocol r evision compatibility  2994 
This feature enables a Device to adjust its protocol layers to a previous SDCI protocol version 2995 
such as for example to the legacy protocol version of a legacy Master or in the future from 2996 
version V(x) to version V(x -n). In the start -up phase the Master System Management  can 2997 
overwrite the Device's inherent protocol RevisionID (RID) in case of discrepancy  with the 2998 
RevisionID supported by the Master . A legacy Master does not write the MasterCommand 2999 
"MasterIdent" (see Table B. 2) and thus the Device can adjust to the legacy protocol (V1.0). 3000 
Revision compatibility support is optional for a Device . 3001 
Devices supporting both V1.0  and V1.1 mode  are permitted   3002 

===PAGE 171===
Interface and System  © IO -Link  – 171 – V1.1.5  
• to use the same predefined parameters , Events , and ErrorTypes  in both modes ; 3003 
• to support Block Parameter ization with full functionality including the Event "DS_UP -3004 
LOAD_REQ". A legacy Master propagates such an Event without any further action . 3005 
  3006 
10.6.4  Visual SDCI indication  3007 
This feature indicates the operational state of the Device 's SDCI interface. The indication of the 3008 
SDCI mode is specified  in 10.10.3 . Indication of the SIO mode is vendor specific and not 3009 
covered by this definition. The function is triggered by the indication of the System Management  3010 
(within all states except  SM_Idle and SM_SIO  in Figure 81). SDCI indication is optional for a 3011 
Device .  3012 
10.6.5  Parameter access locking  3013 
This feature enables a Device  to globally lock or unlock write access to all writ eable Device  3014 
parameters accessible via the SDCI interface ( see B.2.4 ). The locking is triggered by the 3015 
reception of a system parameter "Device Access Locks" ( see Table B. 8). The support for th ese 3016 
function s is optional for a Device . 3017 
NOTE  It is highly recommended not to implement this feature since it will be omitted in future releases.  3018 
10.6.6  Data Storage  locking  3019 
Setting this lock will cause the "State_Property" in Table B. 10 to switch to "Data Storage locked" 3020 
and the Device not to send a DS_UPLOAD_REQ Event.  Support of this function is optional  for 3021 
a Device  if the D ata Storage  mechanism  is implemented . 3022 
NOTE  It is highly recommended not to implement this feature since it will be omitted in future releases.  3023 
10.6.7  Locking  of local parameter entries  3024 
Setting this lock shall have the effect of read only or write protection for local entries at the 3025 
Device (Bit 2 in Table B. 12). Support of this function is optional for a Device , see B.2.4 . 3026 
10.6.8  Locking  of local user interface  3027 
Setting this lock shall have the effect of complete disabling of controls and displays, for example 3028 
shut -down of on-board  human machine interface  such as keypads  on a Device  (Bit 3 in Table 3029 
B.12). Support of this function is optional for a Device . 3030 
10.6.9  Offset time  3031 
The OffsetT ime toffset  is a parameter to be configured by the user  (see B.2.25 ). It determines 3032 
the beginning of the Device 's technology data processing in respect to the start of the M-3033 
sequence  cycle, that means  the beginning of the Master  (port) message. The offset enables  3034 
• Data processing of a Device  to be synchronized with the Master  (port) cycle within certain 3035 
limits ; 3036 
• Data processing of multiple Device s on different Master  ports to be synchronized with one 3037 
another ;  3038 
• Data processing of multiple Device s on different Master  ports to run with a defined offset .  3039 
Figure 92 demon strates the timing of messages in respect to the data processing in Device s. 3040 
 3041

The OffsetTime defines a trigger relative to the start of a n M-sequence  cycle. The support for 3043 
this function is optional for a Device . 3044 
10.6.10  Data Storage concept  3045 
The Data Storage mechanism in a Device  allows to automatically save parameters in the Data 3046 
Storage server of the Master  and to restore them upon Event  notification . Data consistency is 3047 
checked  in either direction within th e Master  and Device . Data Storage  mainly focuses on 3048 
configuration parameters of a Device  set up during commissioning (see 10.4  and 11.4 ). 3049 
10.6.11  Block Parameter  3050 
The Block Parameter transmission f eature  in a Device  allows transfer of parameter sets from a 3051 
PLC program  without checking the consistency single data object by single data object.  The 3052 
validity and consistency check are performed at the end of the Block  Parameter  transmission  3053 
for the entire parameter set . This function mainly focuses on exchange of parameters of a 3054 
Device  to be  set up at runtime ( see 10.3 ). The support of this function is optional for a Device . 3055 
10.7  Device reset options  3056 
10.7.1  Overview  3057 
There are five possibilities for the user to put a Device into a certain defined condition by using 3058 
either  3059 
• Power supply off/on (PowerCycle), or  3060 
• SystemCommand "Device reset"  (128), or  3061 
• SystemCommand "Application reset" (129 ), or  3062 
• SystemCommand "Restore factory settings" (130 ), or  3063 
• SystemCommand "Back to box" (131 ). 3064 
 3065 
Table B. 9 defines which of these SystemCommands are mandatory, highly recommended or 3066 
optional.  3067 
Table 101 provides an overview on impacted items  when performing one of these options . 3068

 3071 
10.7.2  Device reset  3072 
This feature enables a Device to perform a "warm start". It is especially useful, whenever a 3073 
Device needs to be reset to an initial state such as power -on, which means com munication will 3074 
be interrupted.  3075 
This feature is triggered upon reception of SystemCommand "Device reset" (see Table B. 9). 3076 
The ISDU response to this SystemCommand shall be transmitted to the Master after successful 3077 
execution of the requested action. The Device shall wait at least 3 MasterCycle times after the 3078 
last ISDU Response prior to the communication stop.  3079 
The SystemCommand "Device reset" is optional for a Device.   3080 
10.7.3  Application reset  3081 
This feature enables a Device to reset the technology specific application. It is especially useful, 3082 
whenever a technology specific application needs to be set to a predefined operational state 3083 
without communication interruption and a shut -down cycle. Contrary to "Restore factory 3084 
settings" only the application specific parameters are reset to "Default". Each and every 3085 
communication and identification  parameter remains unchanged.  3086 
This feature is triggered upon reception of a SystemCommand "Application reset" (see Table 3087 
B.9). In any case, the ISDU response to this SystemCommand shall be transmitted to the Master 3088 
after successful execution of the requested action.  3089 

===PAGE 174===
V1.1.5  – 174 – Interface and System  © IO -Link  
The SystemCommand "Application reset" is highly recommended for a Device.   3090 
10.7.4  Restore factory settings  3091 
This feature enables a Device to restore parameters to the original delivery status. It is triggered 3092 
upon reception of the SystemCommand "Restore factory settings" (see Table B. 9). The 3093 
DS_UPLOAD_FLAG  (see Table B. 10) and other dynamic parameters such as "ErrorCount" (see 3094 
B.2.18 ), "DeviceStatus" (see B.2.21 ), and "DetailedDeviceStatus" (see B.2.22 ) shall be reset 3095 
when this feature is applied. This does not include vendor specific parameters such as for 3096 
example counters of operating hours.  3097 
NOTE  In this case an existing stored parameter set within the Master will be automatically downloaded into the 3098 
Device after the next communication restart . This can be avoided by using the “Back to box” SystemCommand  (see 3099 
10.7.5 ). 3100 
It is the Device vendor's responsibility to guarantee the correct function under any cir cum-3101 
stances. If any parameter of the Direct Parameter page 1 (see Direct Parameter page  1 in Table 3102 
B.1) is changed during this restore, the communication shall be stopped by the Device to trigger 3103 
a new communication start using the updated communication  and identification  parameters. 3104 
The ISDU response to this SystemCommand shall be transmitted to the Master after successful 3105 
execution of the requested action. The Device shall wait at least 3 MasterCycle times after the 3106 
last ISDU Response prior to the communication stop.   3107 
The SystemCommand "Restore factory settings" is optional for a Device.   3108 
10.7.5  Back -to-box 3109 
This feature enables a Device to restore parameters to the original delivery values without any 3110 
inter action  with upper level mechanisms such as Data Storage or PLC based parame terization. 3111 
It is especially useful, whenever a Device is removed from an already parame teri zed installation 3112 
and reactivated for example as a spare part. If the Device remains in an auto mation application 3113 
beyond the next PowerCycle, all parametrization will be overwritten just as if it were a 3114 
replacement.  3115 
It is triggered upon reception of the SystemCommand "Back -to-box" (see Table B. 9), i.e. the 3116 
Device shall stop and disable communication until next PowerCycle. The ISDU response to this 3117 
SystemCommand shall be transmitted to the Master after successful execution of the requested 3118 
action. The Device shall wait at least 3 MasterCycle times after the last ISDU Response prior 3119 
to the communication stop . All digital signal output drivers shall be disabled and o ptionally the 3120 
Device can visually signal the completion of the action.   3121 
The SystemCommand "Back -to-box" is conditional on the provision of minimum one user 3122 
changeable non -volatile parameter .  3123 
10.7.6  Explanation on impacted items  3124 
The list of impacted items in Table 101  comprises several different parameter types. To explain 3125 
different categories some standardized parameters are assigned.  3126 
• Diagnosis and Status: Comprising the parameters containing the internal Device status like 3127 
DeviceStatus and DetailledDeviceStatus  3128 
• History recorder: Comprising the parameters containing the information regarding the life 3129 
cycle of the Device like Operating hours counter or minimum or maximum ambient 3130 
temperature  3131 
• Technology specific parameter: Comprising the user settings regarding the Device 3132 
functionality like AccessLocks  or profiled functional parameters  like setpoints  3133 
• Identification/tags: Comprising the parameter s which allow the customer to identify the 3134 
specific Device by unique identifier like ApplicationSpecificTag, FunctionTag, and 3135 
LocationTag  3136 
10.8  Device design rules and constraints  3137 
10.8.1  General  3138 
In addition to the protocol definitions in form of state, sequence, activity, and timing diagrams 3139 
some more rules and constraints are required to define the behavior of the Device s. An overview 3140 

===PAGE 175===
Interface and System  © IO -Link  – 175 – V1.1.5  
of the major protocol variables scattered all over the standard  is concentrated in Table 102 with 3141 
associated references.  3142 
10.8.2  Process Data  3143 
The process communication  channel transmits the cyclic Process Data  without  any interference 3144 
of the On-request  Data  communication  channel s. Process Data  exchange starts automatically 3145 
whenever the Device is switched into the OPERATE state via message from the Master .  3146 
The format of the transmitted data is Device  specific and varies  from no data octets up to 32 3147 
octets in each communication direction.  3148 
Recommendations:  3149 
• Data structures should be suitable for use by PLC applications.  3150 
• It is highly recommended to comply with the rules  in F.3.3  and in [6]. 3151 
See 10.2  for details on the indication of valid or invalid Process Data.   3152 
10.8.3  Communication loss  3153 
It is the responsibility of the Device designer to define the appropriate behaviour of the Device 3154 
in case communication with the Master is lost (transition T 10 in Figure 44 handles detection of 3155 
the communication loss, while 10.2  defines resulting Device actions ). 3156 
NOTE  This is especially important for actuators such as valves or motor management.  3157 
10.8.4  Direct Parameter  3158 
The Direct Parameter page communication provides no handshake mechanism to ensure proper 3159 
reception or validity of the transmitted parameters. The Direct Parameter page can only be 3160 
accessed single octet by single octet (Subindex) or as a whole (16 octets).  The consistency of 3161 
parameters larger than 1 octet cannot be guaranteed.  3162 
The parameters from the Direct Parameter page cannot be saved and restored via the Data 3163 
Storage mechanism.  3164 
10.8.5  ISDU communication  channel  3165 
The ISDU communication  channel  provides a powerful means for the transmission of 3166 
parameters and commands ( see Clause B.2).  3167 
The following rules shall be considered when using this channel  (see Figure 7). 3168 
• Index 0 is not accessible via the ISDU communication  channel.  The access is redirected  by 3169 
the Master  to the Direct Parameter page 1 using  the page communication channel . 3170 
• Index 1 is not accessible via the ISDU communication channel . The access is redirected  by 3171 
the Master  to the Direct Parameter page 2 using  the page communication channel . 3172 
• Index 3 cannot be accessed by a PLC  application  program . The access is limited to the 3173 
Master  application only (Data Storage).  3174 
• After reception of an  ISDU  request from the Master  the Device shall respond  within 5  000 ms 3175 
(see Table 102). Any violation causes the Master to abandon the current task . 3176 
• Parameters with attribute write -only (W) shall be treated like a SystemCommand. Only basic 3177 
data types are permitted.   3178 
10.8.6  Device ID rules related to Device variants  3179 
Devices with a certain DeviceID and VendorID shall not deviate in communication and functional 3180 
behavior. This applies for sensors and actuators. Those Devices may vary for example in   3181 
• cable lengths,  3182 
• housing materials,  3183 
• mounting mechanisms,  3184 
• other features, and environmental conditions.  3185 

===PAGE 176===
V1.1.5  – 176 – Interface and System  © IO -Link

10.9  IO Device description (IODD)  3190 
An IODD (I /O Device Description) is a file that provides all the necessary properties to establish 3191 
communication and the necessary parameters and their boundaries to establish the desired 3192 
function of a sensor or actuator.  3193 
An IODD (I /O Device Description) is a file that formally describes a Device.   3194 
An IODD file shall be provided for each Device and  shall include all information necessary to 3195 
support this standard.  3196 
The IODD can be used by engineering tools  for PLCs and/or Master s for the purpose of 3197 
identification, configuration, definition  of data structures for Process Data  exchange, 3198 
parameterization , and diagnosis  decoding  of a  particular Device.  3199 
NOTE  Details of the IODD language to describe a Device can be found in [6] . 3200 
10.10  Device diagnosis  3201 
10.10.1  Concepts  3202 
This standard  provides only most common EventCodes in D.2. It is the purpose of these 3203 
common diagnosis informations to enable an operator or maintenance person to take  fast 3204 
remedial measures without deep knowledge of the Device 's technology. Thus, the text 3205 
associated with a particular EventCode shall always contain a corrective instruction together 3206 
with the diagnosis information.   3207 
Fieldbus -Master -Gateways tend to only map few EventCodes to the upper system level. 3208 
Usually , vendor specific EventCodes defined via the IODD can only be decoded into readable 3209 
instructions via a Port and Device Configurat ion Tool (PDCT)  or specific vendor tool  using the 3210 
IODD . 3211 

===PAGE 177===
Interface and System  © IO -Link  – 177 – V1.1.5  
Condensed  information of the Device's "state of health" can be retrieved  from the parameter 3212 
"DeviceStatus " ( see B.2.21 ). Whenever an Event appears , the DetailedDeviceStatus contains 3213 
this Event until  it disappears, see B.2.22 . Table 103 provides an overview of the various 3214 
possibilities for Devices and shows examples of consumers for this information.   3215 
If implemented, it is also possible to read the number of faults since power -on or reset via the 3216 
parameter " ErrorCount " (see B.2.18 ) and more information in case of profile Devices via the 3217 
parameter "DetailedDevice Status " (see B.2.22 ). 3218 
NOTE  Profile sp ecific values for the "DetailedDevice Status" are given in  [7] . 3219 
A Device may  provide additional "deep" technology specific diagnosis information  in the form 3220 
of Device  specific parameters  (see Table B. 8) that can be retrieved via port and Device 3221 
configura tion  tools for Masters or via vendor specific tools.  Usually, only experts or service 3222 
personnel of the vendor are able to draw conclusions from  this information.  3223

10.10.2  Events  3226 
MODE values shall be assigned as follows (see A.6.4  ): 3227 
• Events of TYPE  "Error " shall use the M ODEs  "Event appears  / disappears"  3228 
• Event s of TYPE "Warning " shall use the M ODEs " Event appears / disappears"  3229 
• Events of TYPE "Notification " shall use the M ODE "Event single shot " 3230 
The following requirements  apply:  3231 
• All Event s already placed in the Event  queue are discarded by the Event Dispatcher  when 3232 
communicati on is interrupted or cancelled.  Once  communication  resume d, the technology 3233 
specific application is responsible for proper reporting of the current Event  causes.  3234 
• It is the responsibility of the Event Dispatcher to control the "Event appear s" and "Event 3235 
disappears" flow. Once the Event Dispatcher has sent an Event with MODE "Event appears" 3236 
for a given EventCode, it shall not send it again for the same EventCode before it has sent 3237 
an Event with MODE "Event disappears" for this same EventCode.  3238 
• Each Event shall use static  mode, type,  and instance  attribute s. 3239 
• Each vendor specific EventCode shall be uniquely assigned to one of the TYPEs (Error, 3240 
Warning, or Notification).  3241 
• Each appearing Event ( "Warning " or "Error ") shall change the DeviceStatus from 3242 
"0: Device  is operating  properl y" to any other  valid  value.  3243 
In order to prevent the diagnosis communication  channel  (see Figure 7) from being flooded, the 3244 
following requirements apply : 3245 
• The same diagnosis information shall not be reported at less than 1 s intervals . This means 3246 
that the Event Dispatcher shall not invoke the AL_Event service with the same EventCode 3247 
and EventQualifier more often than once per second. This measure avoids frequent 3248 
repetitions of Events.   3249 
• The Event Dispatcher shall not issue an "Event disappears" less than 50 ms after the 3250 
corresponding "Event appears".  3251 
• Subsequent incidents of errors or warnings with the same root cause shall be  disregarded , 3252 
that means one root cause shall lead to a single  error or warning.  3253 
• The Event Dispatcher shall invoke the AL_Event service with an EventCount equal  one.  3254 
• Errors are prioritized over W arnings.  3255 
Figure 93 shows how two successive errors are processed, and the corresponding flow of 3256 
"Event appears" / "Event disappears" Event s for each error.  3257 
 3258 
Figure 93 – Event flow in case of successive errors  3259

11.1  Overview  3280 
11.1.1  Positioning of Master and Gateway Applications  3281 
In 0 the domain of the SDCI technology within the automation hierarchy  is already illustrated . 3282 
Figure 95 shows  the recommended  relationship between the SDCI technology and a fieldbus 3283 
technology.  Even though this may be the major use case in practice, this does not automati cally 3284 
imply that the SDCI technology depends on the integration into fieldbus systems. It can also be 3285 
directly integrated into PLC systems, industrial PC, or other automation  systems with out 3286 
fieldbus communication in between.  3287 
For the sake of preferably uniform behavior of Masters, Figure 95 shows a Standardized Master 3288 
Interface (SMI) as layer in between the Master and the Gateway Applications or embedded 3289 
systems on top. This Standardized Master Interface is intended to serve also the safety system 3290 
extensions as well as the wireless system extensions. In case of FS -Masters, attention shall be 3291 
payed to the fact, that this SMI in some aspects  requires implementation according to safety 3292 
standards.  3293 
The Standardized Master Interface is specified in this clause via services and data objects 3294 
similar to the other layers (PL, DL, and AL) in this document. It is designed using few uniform 3295 
base structures that both upper layer fieldbus and upper layer IT systems can use in an efficient 3296 
manner: push ("write"), pull ("read"), push/pull ("write/read"), and indication ("Event").  3297 
The specification of Gateway Applications is not subject of this document. Designers shall 3298 
observe the realtime requirements of control functions and safety functions in case of 3299 
concurrent Gateway Applications (see 13.2 ).   3300 

===PAGE 181===
Interface and System  © IO -Link  – 181 – V1.1.5  
 3301 
NOTE  Blue and orange shaded areas indicate features specified in this standard  except those for functional safety 3302 
(FS) and wireless (W)  3303

The Master applications are located on top of the Master structure and consist of:  3309 
• Configuration Manager (CM), which transforms the user configuration assignments into port 3310 
set-ups;  3311 
• On-request Data Exchange (ODE), which provides for example acyclic parameter access;  3312 
• Data Storage (DS) mechanism, which can be used to save and restore the Device 3313 
parameters;  3314 
• Diagnosis Unit (DU), which routes Events from the AL to the Data Storage unit or the 3315 
gateway application;  3316 
• Process Data Exchange (PDE), building the bridge to upper level automation instruments.  3317 
 3318 
They are accessible by the gateway applications (and others) via the Standardized Master 3319 
Interface (SMI) and its services/methods.  3320 
These services and corresponding functions are specified in an abstract manner within clauses 3321 
11.2.2  to 11.2.22  and Annex  E. 3322 
Master applications are described in detail in clauses 11.3  to 11.7 . The Configuration Mana ger 3323 
(CM) and the Data Storage mechanism (DS) require special coordination with respect to On -3324 
request Data.  3325

11.2.2  Structure of SMI service arguments  3344 
The SMI service arguments contain a fixed structure of standard elements , which are 3345 
characterized in the following.  3346 
ClientID  3347 
Gateway Applications may use the SMI services concurrently as clients of the SMI (see 11.2.3 ). 3348 
Thus, SMI services will assign a unique ClientID to each individual client. It is the responsibility 3349 
of the Gateway Application(s) to coordinate these SMI service activities and to route responses 3350 
to the calling client. The maximum number of concurrent clients is Master specific.  3351 
Data type:  Unsigned8  3352 
Permitted values:  1 to vendor specific maximum number of concurrent clients. "0" is solely 3353 
used for broadcast purposes in case of indications, see 11.2.15  and 11.2.16 . 3354 
PortNumber  3355 
Each SMI service contains the port number in case of an addressed port object (job) or in case 3356 
of a triggered port object (event).  3357 
Data type:  Unsigned8  3358 
Permitted values:  1 to MaxNumberOfPorts. "0" is solely used to address the entire Master 3359 
(see 11.2.4 ). 3360 
ExpArgBlockID  3361 
This element specifies the expected  ArgBlock ID to carry the response data of a service request . 3362 
The IDs are defined in Table E.1. 3363 
Data type:  Unsigned16  3364 
Permitted values:  1 to to 65535  3365 
RefArgBlockID  3366 
Within results, t his element specifies  the ID of the Argblock sent by the service request . The 3367 
IDs are defined in Table E.1. 3368 
Data type:  Unsigned16  3369 
Permitted values:  1 to to 65535  3370 
ArgBlockLength  3371 
This element specifies the total length of the subsequent  ArgBlock . Vendor specific exten sions  3372 
are not permitted . 3373 
Data type:  Unsigned16  3374 
Permitted values:  2 to to 65535  3375 
ArgBlock  3376 
All SMI services contain an ArgBlock characterized by an ArgBlockID and its description.  3377 
Service results provide the ArgBlock associated to the ExpArgBlockID, which is part of this 3378 
ArgBlock.  The possibly variable length of the ArgBlock is pre defined through definition in this 3379 
document . 3380 
Pairs of ExpArgBlock/ RefArgBlock and ArgBlock ID within one SMI structure  shall be unique. 3381 
Detailed coding of the Arg Blocks is specified in Annex  E. ArgBlock types and their ArgBlockIDs 3382 
are defined in Table E.1. Service errors are listed at each individual service and in C.4. 3383 
11.2.3  Concurrency and prioritization of SMI services  3384 
The following rules apply for concurrency of SMI services when accessing attributes:  3385 

===PAGE 185===
Interface and System  © IO -Link  – 185 – V1.1.5  
• All SMI services with different PortNumber access different port objects (disjoint opera -3386 
tions) ; 3387 
• Different SMI services using the same PortNumber access different attributes/methods of a 3388 
port object (concurrent operations) ; 3389 
• Identical SMI services using the same PortNumber and different ClientIDs access identical 3390 
attributes concurrently (consistency) . 3391 
The following rules apply for SMI services when accessing methods:  3392 
• SMI services for methods using different PortNumbers access different port objects (disjoint 3393 
operations) ; 3394 
• SMI services for methods using the same PortNumber and different ClientIDs create job 3395 
instances and will be processed in the order of their arrival ( n Client concurrency) ; 3396 
• SMI_ ParamWriteBatch (ArgBlock " DeviceBatch ") shall be treated as a job instance that  shall 3397 
not be interrupted by any SMI_DeviceWrite or SMI_DeviceRead service .  3398 
Prioritization of SMI services within the Standardized Master Interface is not performed. All 3399 
services accessing methods will be processed in the order of their arrival (first come, first 3400 
serve).  3401 
11.2.4  SMI_MasterIdentification  3402 
So far, an explicit identification of a Master did not have priority in SDCI since gateway appli -3403 
cations usually provided hard -coded identification and maintenance information as required by 3404 
the fieldbus system. Due to the requirement "one Master Tool (PCDT) fits different Master 3405 
brands", corresponding new Master Tools shall be able to connect to Masters providing an SMI. 3406 
For that purpose, the SMI_MasterIdentification service has been created. It allows Mas ter Tools 3407 
to adjust to individual Master brands and types, if a particular fieldbus gateway pro vides the 3408 
SMI services in a uniform accessible coding (see clause 13). A class of Masters with a certain 3409 
MasterID and VendorID shall not deviate in communication and functional behavior (Master 3410 
type identification). Table 106 shows t he service SMI_Master Identi fication.  3411

11.2.12  SMI_ ParamWriteBatch  3754 
This service allows for the "push" transfer of a large number of con sistent Device objects  via 3755 
multiple ISDUs. Table 114 shows the structure of the service.  The following rules apply:  3756 
• The service transfers the ArgBlock "DeviceParBatch" to the Master that conveys the content 3757 
object by object  to the Device via AL_Write  (ISDU).  3758 
• The same ArgBlock structure is returned as Result (+). However, a value "0x0000" indicates 3759 
success of a particular AL_Write or an ISDU ErrorType of a failed AL_Write  instead of a 3760 
parameter record . 3761 
• Result ( -) is only returned in case of a failing service via "JobError".  3762 
NOTE1   This service supposes use of Block Parameterization and sufficient buffer ressources  3763 
NOTE2   This service may have unexpected duration   3764 
This service is  optional. Availability is indicated via Master identification (see Table E. 2)  3765

11.2.13  SMI_ParamReadBatch  3809 
This service allows for the  "pull"  transfer of a large number of con sistent Device parameters via 3810 
multiple ISDUs. Table 114 shows the structure of the service.  The following rules apply:  3811 
• The service transfers the ArgBlock "IndexList" to the Master that transforms the content 3812 
entry by entry into AL_Read (ISDU) to the Device.  3813 
• The corresponding ArgBlock "DeviceParBatch is returned as Result (+). In case of a 3814 
successful AL_Read of an object, the corresponding parameter  record or an ISDU ErrorType 3815 
of a failed AL_ Read  instead of a parameter record  is returned . 3816 
• Result ( -) is only returned in case of a failing service via "JobError".  3817 
NOTE1   This service supposes use of Block Parameterization and sufficient buffer ressources  3818 
NOTE2   This service may have unexpected duration  3819

11.3  Configuration Manager (CM)  4220 
11.3.1  Coordination of Master applications  4221 
Figure 99 illustrates the coordination between Master applications. Main responsibility is 4222 
assigned to the Configuration Manager (CM), who initializes port start -ups and who starts or 4223 
stops the other Master applications depending on a respective port state.  4224 
 4225 
Figure 99 – Coordination of Master applications  4226 
Internal variables and Events controlling Master applications are listed in Table 125. 4227 
Table 125 – Internal variables and Events controlling Master applications  4228 
Internal Variable  Definition  
DS_Startup  This variable triggers the Data Storage (DS) state machine causing an Upload 
or Download of Device parameters if required (see 11.4 ).  
DS_Ready  This variable indicates the Data Storage has been accomplished successfully; 
operating mode is CFGCOM or AUTOCOM (see 9.2.2.2 )  
DS_Fault  This variable indicates the Data Storage has been aborted due to a fault.   
DS_Delete  Any relevant  change of port configuration leads to a deletion of the stored data 
set in the Data Storage.   
DS_Change  This variable indicates a content change of Data Storage  triggered by service 
SMI_ParServToDS . 
DS_Upload  This variable triggers the Data Storage state machine in the Master due to the 
special Event "DS_UPLOAD_REQ" from the Device.  
OD_Start  This variable enables On -request Data access via AL_Read and AL_Write.

Internal Variable  Definition  
OD_Stop  This variable indicates that On -request Data access via AL_Read and AL_Write 
is acknowledged with a negative response to the gateway application.   
OD_Block  Data Storage upload and download actions disable the On -request Data access 
through AL_Read or AL_Write. Access by the gateway application is denied.  
OD_Unblock  This variable enables On -request Data access via AL_Read or AL_Write.  
DU_Start  This variable enables the Diagnosis Unit to propagate remote (Device) Events 
to the gateway application.   
DU_Stop  This variable indicates that the Device Events are not propagated to the 
gateway application and not acknowledged. Available Events are blocked until 
the DU is enabled again.  
PD_Start  This variable enables the Process Data exchange with the gateway application.  
PD_Stop  This variable disables the Process Data exchange with the gateway application.  
 4229 
Restart of a port is basically driven by two activities:  4230 
• SMI_PortConfiguration service (Port parameter setting and start -up or changes and restart 4231 
of a port)  4232 
• SMI_ParServ ToDS  service (Download of Data Storage data if Data Storage is activated )  4233 
 4234 
The Configuration Manager (CM) is launched upon reception of a "SMI_PortConfiguration" 4235 
service. The elements of parameter "PortConfigList" are stored in non-volatile memory within 4236 
the Master. The service "SMI_ReadbackPortConfiguration" allows for checking correct storage.  4237 
CM uses the values of ArgBlock "PortConfigList", initializes the port start -up in case of value 4238 
changes and conditionally empties the Data Storage via "DS_Delete" or checks emptiness (see 4239 
Figure 99).  4240 
A gateway application can poll the actual port state via "SMI_PortStatus" to check whether the 4241 
expected port state is reached. In case of fault this service provides corresponding information.  4242 
After successfully setting up the port, CM starts the Data Storage mechanism and returns via 4243 
parameter element "PortStatusInfo" either "OPERATE" or "PORT_FAULT" to the gateway 4244 
application.  4245 
In case of "OPERATE", CM activates the state machines of the associated Master applica tions 4246 
Diagnosis Unit (DU), On -request Data Exchange (ODE), and Process Data Exchange (PDE).  4247 
In case of a fault in SM_PortMode such as COMP_FAULT, REVISION_FAULT, or 4248 
SERNUM_FAULT according to 9.2.3 , CM activates the state machines of the associated Master 4249 
applications Diagnosis Unit (DU) and On -request Data Exchange (ODE) .  4250 
Figure 100 illustrates the start -up of a port via SMI_ PortConfiguration service  in a sequence 4251 
diagram . 4252 

===PAGE 210===
V1.1.5  – 210 – Interface and System  © IO -Link  
 4253 
Figure 100 – Sequence diagram of start -up via Configuration Manager  4254 
  4255 
11.3.2  State machine of the Configuration Manager  4256 
Figure 101 shows the state machine of the Configuration Manager. In general, states and 4257 
transitions correspond to those of the message handler: STARTUP, PREOPERATE (fault or 4258 
Data Storage), and at the end OPERATE. Dedicated "SM_PortMode" services are driving the 4259 
transitions (see 9.2.2.4 ). A special state is related to SIO mode DI or DO.   4260 
Configuration Manager can receive the information COMLOST from Port x Handler through 4261 
"SM_PortMode" at any time. It also can receive a  service "SMI_PortConfiguration" from the 4262 
gateway application with changed values in "PortConfigList" also at any time (see 11.2.5 ). 4263 
It can also receive a Data  Storage object  with a changed parameter set via  service 4264 
"SMI_ParServToDS " from the gateway application trigger ing action in the Configuration 4265 
Manager  if Data Storage is activated . 4266 
Port x is started/restarted in all cases.  4267 
Figure 101 together with Table 126  also shows transitions leading to corresponding changes in 4268 
"PortStatusInfo" of ArgBlock "PortStatusList" (see Table E. 4). Based on these transitions, 4269 
Events are triggered via SMI_PortEvent. For details see Clause D.3.  4270

11.4  Data Storage (DS)  4283 
11.4.1  Overview  4284 
Data Storage between Master and Device is specified within this standard , whereas the 4285 
adjacent upper Data Storage mechanisms depend on the individual fieldbus or system. The 4286 
Device  holds a standardized set of objects providing parameters for Data Storage , memory size 4287 
requirements, control and state information of the Data Storage mechanism. Changes of Data 4288 
Storage parameter sets are detectable via the "Parameter Checksum" ( see 10.4.8 ). 4289 
11.4.2  DS data object  4290 
The structure of a Data Storage data object is specified  in Table G.1. 4291 
The Master  shall always hold the header information (Parameter Checksum, VendorID , and 4292 
Device ID) for the purpose of checking and control. The object information (objects 1… n) will be 4293 
stored within the non -volatile memory part of the Master  (see Annex  G). Prior to a down load of 4294 
the Data Storage data object (parameter block), the Master  will check the consistency of the 4295 
header information with the particular Device.  4296 
The maximum permitted size of the Data Storage data object is 2048  octets.  It is mandatory for 4297 
Masters to provide at least this memory space per port .  4298 
11.4.3  Backup and Restore  4299 
Gateways are able to retrieve a port's current Data Storage object out of the Master using the 4300 
service "SMI_DSToParServ", see 11.2.8 . 4301 
In return, gateways are also able to write a port's current Data Storage object into the Master 4302 
using the service "SMI_ParServ ToDS " (see 11.2.9 ). This causes under certain conditions an 4303 
implicit restart of the Device and activation of the parameters within the Device  (see 11.3.2 ).  4304

SM: CheckIdentity_4  Check Device identification (DeviceID, VendorID) against parameter  set within the Data 
Storage  (see Table G.2). Empty  content does not lead to a fault.  
SM: Check MemSize_5  Check data set size (Index 3, Subindex 3) agains t available Master storage size  
SM: CheckUpload_6  Check for DS_UPLOAD_FLAG within the DataStorageIndex (see Table B. 10) 
SM: Upload_7  Submachine for the upload actions

designer  to implement a validity mechanism according to the chosen use cases

G.2

the higher -level  indication DS_Fault.

SM: Write_Parameter_18  Wait until write of one parameter of the data set into the Device is accomplished.  
SM: Download_Done_19  Download completed. Read back "Parameter Checksum" from the DataStorageIndex 
according to Table B. 10. Save this value in the stored data set according to Table G.2.  
SM: Download_Fault_20  Prepare Download_Fault indication from "Device_Error" and "COM_ERROR" as input 
for the higher -level  indication DS_Fault.

11.4.5  Parameter selection for Data Storage  4345 
The Device  designer defines the parameters that are part of the Data Storage mechanism.  4346 
The IODD marks all parameters not included in Data Storage with the attribute " exclu -4347 
dedFrom DataStorage". However, the Data Storage mechanism shall not consider the infor -4348 
mation from the IODD but rather the Parameter List read out from the Device.  4349 
11.5  On-request Data exchange (ODE)  4350 
Figure 109 shows the state machine of the Master's On -request Data Exchange. This behave -4351 
iour is mandatory for a Master.  4352 
The gateway application is able to read On -request Data (OD) from the Device via the service 4353 
"SMI_DeviceRead". This service is directly mapped to service AL_ Read  with Port, Index, and 4354 
Subindex (see 8.2.2.1 ). 4355 
The gateway application is able to write On -request Data (OD) to the Device via the service 4356 
"SMI_DeviceWrite". This service is directly mapped to service AL_W rite  with Port, Index, and 4357 
Subindex (see 8.2.2.2).  4358 
During an active data transmission of the Data Storage mechanism, all On -request Data re -4359 
quests are blocked.  4360

11.6  Diagnosis Unit (DU)  4369 
11.6.1  General  4370 
The Diagnosis Unit (DU) routes Device or Port specific Events via the SMI_DeviceEvent and 4371 
the SMI_PortEvent service to the gateway application (see Figure 99). These Events primarily 4372 
contain diagnosis information. The structure corresponds to the AL_Event in 8.2.2.11  with 4373 
Instance, Mode, Type, Origin, and Event Code.  4374 
Additionally, the DU generates a Device or port specific diagnosis status that can be retrieved 4375 
by the SMI_PortStatus service in PortStatusList (see Table E. 4 and 11.6.4 ). 4376 
11.6.2  Device specific Events  4377 
The SMI_DeviceEvent service provides Device specific Events directly to the gateway appli -4378 
cation.  The special DS_UPLOAD_REQ Event (see 10.4  and Table D. 1) of a Device shall be 4379 
redirect ted to the common Master application Data Storage. Those Events are acknowledged 4380 
by the DU itself and not propagated via SMI_DeviceEvent to the gateway.  4381 
Device diagnosis information flooding is avoided by flow control as shown in Figure 110, which 4382 
allows for only one Event per Device to be propagated via SMI_DeviceEvent to the gateway 4383 
application at a time.  4384 

===PAGE 223===
Interface and System  © IO -Link  – 223 – V1.1.5  
 4385 
Figure 110 – DeviceEvent flow control  4386 
11.6.3  Port specific Events  4387 
The SMI_PortEvent service provides also port specific Events directly to the gateway appli -4388 
cation. Those Events are similarly characterized by Instance = Application, Source = Master, 4389 
Type = Error or Warning  or Notification , and Mode Event appears or disappears  or single shot 4390 
(see A.6.4 ). Usually, only one port Event at a time is pending as shown in Figure 111. 4391 
 4392 
Figure 111 – Port  Event flow control  4393 
The following rules apply:  4394 
• It is not required to send  disappearing Port Events  in case of Device communication interrupt 4395 
(communication restart);  4396 
• Once communication resumed, the gateway client is responsible for proper reporting of the 4397 
current Event causes.  4398 
Port specific Events are specified in  Annex  D.3. 4399 
11.6.4  Dynamic diagnosis status  4400 
DU generates the diagnosis status by collecting all appearing DeviceEvents and PortEvents 4401 
continuously in a buffer. Any disappearing Event will cause the DU to remove the corres ponding 4402 
Event with the same EventCode from the buffer. Thus, the buffer represents an actual image of 4403 
the consolidated diagnosis status, which can be taken over as diagnosis entries with in the 4404 
PortStatusList (see Table E. 4). 4405 
After COMLOST  and during Device startup the buffer will be deleted.  4406 
11.6.5  Best practice recommendations  4407 
Main goal for diagnosis information is to alert  an operator in an efficient manner. That means:  4408 
• no diagnosis information flooding  4409

• diagnosis information sha ll provide information on how to maintain or repair the affected 4412

Within SDCI, diagnosis information o n Devices is conveyed to the Master via Events consist ing 4417 
of EventQualifiers and EventCodes ( see A.6). The associated human readable text is available 4418 
for standardized EventCodes within this standard  (see Annex  D) and for vendor specific 4419 
EventCodes within the associated IODD file of a Device.  4420

diagnosis definitions within the gateway application.  4422

11.7  PD Exchange ( PDE)  4426 
11.7.1  General  4427 
The Process Data Exchange provides the transmission of Process Data between the gateway 4428 
application and the connected Device.  4429 
The Standard Master Interface (SMI) comes with the following three services for the gateway 4430 
application:  4431 
• SMI_PDIn allows for reading input Process Data from the InBuffer together with Quality 4432 
Information (PQI), see 11.2.17  4433 
• SMI_PDOut allows for writing output Process Data to the OutBuffer, see 11.2.18  4434 
• SMI_PDInOut allows for reading output Process Data from the OutBuffer and reading input 4435 
Process Data from the InBuffer within one cycle, see 11.2.19   4436 
After an established communication and Data Storage, the port is ready for any On -request 4437 
Data (ODE) transfers. Process Data exchange is enabled whenever the specific port or all ports 4438 
are switched to the OPERATE mode.  4439

Figure 99 shows how the Master application "Process Data Exchange" (PDE) is related to the 4442

parameter Input DataLength, and  the input Process Data . 4449

anytime the input data are read . Figure 114 illustrates the structure of this octet.    4454

11.7.3  Process Data output mapping  4477 
11.7.3.1  Port Modes "IOL_MANUAL" or "IOL_AUTOSTART"  4478 
Master application "Process Data Exchange" (PDE) is responsible for the cyclic transfer of 4479 
output data using the service s "AL_SetOutput" (see 8.2.2.10 ) and "AL_Control" (see 8.2.2.12 ). 4480 
Both shall be synchronized for consistency . 4481 
A gateway application can write data via the service "SMI_PDOut" into the OutBuffer  (see 4482 
11.2.18 ). Figure 115 illustrates the principles of Process Data Output mapping  and the content 4483 
of the ArgBlock of this service (see E.11 ) consisting of the ArgBlockID, the Output Enable bit, 4484 
the parameter OutputDataLength, and the output Process Data .  4485 
An ErrorType 0x4034 – Incorrect ArgBlock length  will be returned if length does not add up to 4486 
Process Data Out plus four  octets  (see C.4.9 ).  4487 
 4488 
Figure 115 – Principles of Process Data Output mapping  4489 
At state OPERATE the Process Data Out are cyclicly copied to output data starting at offset 4490 
"3". 4491 
The OutBuffer is expanded by an octet "OE" (Output Enable) at offset  "2". Bit 0 indicates the 4492 
validity of the Process Data Out. "0" means invalid, "1" means valid data. A change of this Bit 4493 
from "0" to "1" will launch an AL_Control with "PDout valid". A change of this Bit from "1" to "0" 4494 
will launch an AL_Control with "PDout invalid". See "OE detect" in Figure 115. 4495 
11.7.3.2  Port Mode: "DO_C/Q"  4496 
In this Port Mode octet 0, Bit 0 of the Process Data Out in the OutBuffer will be mapped into 4497 
the signal status of DO_C/Q (see Figure 115). 4498

12 Holistic view on Data Storage  4524 
12.1  User point of view  4525 
In this clause the Data Storage mechanism is described from a holistic user's point of view as 4526 
best practice pattern. This is in contrast to clause 10.4  and 11.4  where Device and Master are 4527 
described separately and each with more features then used within th e recommended concept  4528 
herein after . 4529 
12.2  Operations and preconditions  4530 
12.2.1  Purpose and objectives  4531 
Main purpose of the IO -Link Data Storage mechanism is the replacement of obviously defect 4532 
Devices or Masters by spare parts (new or used) without using configuration, parameter zation, 4533 
or other tools. The scenarios and associated preconditions are described in the following 4534 
clauses.  4535 
12.2.2  Preconditions for the activation of the Data Storage mechanism  4536 
The following preconditions shall be observed prior to the usage of Data Storage:  4537 
a) Data Storage is only available for Devices and Masters implemented according to this 4538 
document ( ≥ V1.1 ). 4539 
b) The Inspection Level of that Master port , the Device is connected to shall be adjusted to 4540 
"type compatible" (corresponds to "TYPE_COMP" within Table 80) 4541 
c) The Backup Level of that Master port , the Device is connected to shall be either 4542 
"Backup/Restore" or "Restore", which corresponds to DS_Enabled in 11.4.4 . See 12.4  4543 
within this document for details on Backup Level.  4544 
12.2.3  Preconditions for the types of Devices to be replaced  4545 
After activation of a Backup Level (Data Storage mechanism) a "faulty" Device can be replaced 4546 
by a type equivalent or compatible other Device. In some exceptional cases, for example non -4547 
calibrated Devices, a user manipulation can be required such as teach -in, to guarantee the 4548 
same functionality and performance.  4549 
Thus, two classes  of Devices exist in respect to exchangeability, which shall be described in 4550 
the user manual of the particular Device:  4551 
Data Storage class 1: automatic DS  4552 
The configured Device supports Data Storage in such a manner that the replacement Device 4553 
plays the role of its predecessor fully automatically and with the same performance.  4554 
Data Storage class 2: semi -automatic DS  4555 
The configured Device supports Data Storage in such a manner that the replacement Device 4556 
requires user manipulation such as teach -in prior to operation with the same performance.  4557 
The Data Storage class shall be described in the user manual of the Device.  Device designer 4558 
is responsible in case of class 2 to prevent from dangerous system restart after Device 4559 
replacement, at least via descriptions within the user manual.   4560 
12.2.4  Preconditions for the parameter sets  4561 
Each Device operates with the configured set of active parameters. The associated set of 4562 
backup parameters stored within the system (Master and upper level system, for example PLC) 4563 
can be different from the set of active parameters (see Figure 118). 4564 

===PAGE 230===
V1.1.5  – 230 – Interface and System  © IO -Link  
 4565 
Figure 118 – Active and backup parameter  4566 
A replacement of the Device in operation will result in overwriting the active  parameter  set with 4567 
the backup parameters in the newly connected Device . 4568 
12.3  Commissioning  4569 
12.3.1  On-line commissioning  4570 
Usually, the Devices are configured and parameterized along with the configuration and 4571 
parameterization of the fieldbus and PLC system with the help of engineering tools. After the 4572 
user assigned values to the parameters, they are downloaded into the Device and become 4573 
active parameters. Upon the system command  "ParamDownloadStore" , these parameters are 4574 
uploaded (copied) into the Data Storage within the Master, which in turn will initiate a backup 4575 
of all its parameters depending on the features of the upper level system.  4576 
12.3.2  Off-site commissioning  4577 
Another possibility is the configuration and parameterization of Devices with the help of extra 4578 
tools such as "USB -Masters" and the IODD of the Device away (off -site) from the machine/ 4579 
facility (see Figure 119). 4580 
The USB -Master tool will mark  the parameter set after configuration, parameterization, and 4581 
vali dation (to become "active") via DS_UPLOAD_FLAG  (see Table 131 and Table B. 10). After 4582 
instal lation in to the machine/facility these parameters are uploaded (copied) automatically into 4583 
the Data Storage within the Master (backup).  4584 
 4585 
Figure 119 – Off-site commissioning  4586

requirements for backup of parameters via Data Storage can be identified:  4590

The following clauses describe the phases in detail.  4610 
12.4.3  Commissioning ("Disable")  4611 
Data Storage is disabled in Master port configuration , where configurations, parame teri zations, 4612 
and PLC programs are fine -tuned, tested, and verified. This includes the involved IO -Link 4613 
Masters and Devices. Usually, repeated saving (uploading) of the active Device para meters 4614 
makes no sense in this phase. As a consequence, the replacement of Master and De vices with 4615 
automatic/semi -automatic Data Storage is not supported.  4616 
12.4.4  Production ("Backup/Restore")  4617 
Data Storage in Master port configuration will be enabled. Current active parameters within the 4618 
Device will be copied/saved as backup parameters. Device replacement with auto matic/semi -4619 
automatic Data Storage is now supported via download/copy of the backup pa rameters to the 4620 
Device and thus turning them into active parameters.  4621 
Criteria for the particular copy activities are listed in Table 131. These criteria are the con ditions 4622 
to trigger a copy process of the active parameters to the backup parameters, thus ensuring the 4623 
consistency of these two sets.  4624

12.4.5  Production ("Restore")  4627 
Data Storage in Master port configuration is enabled. However, only DS_Download operation 4628 
is available. This means, unintended overwriting of Data Storage within the Master is prohibited.   4629 
Any changes of the active parameters through teach -in, tool based para meteri zation, or local 4630 
parameterization will  lead to a Data Storage Event, and State Property "DS_UPLOAD_FLAG " 4631 
will  be set in the Device.  4632 
In back -up level Production ("Restore") the Master shall ignore this flag and shall issue a 4633 
DS_Download to overwrite the changed parameters.  4634

to trigger a copy process of the active parameters to the backup parameters, thus ensuring the 4636 
consistency of these two sets.  4637

12.5  Use cases  4640 
12.5.1  Device replacement (@ "Backup/Restore")  4641 
The stored (saved) set of back -up parameters overwrites the active parameters (e.g. factory 4642 
settings) within the replaced compatible Device of same type. This one operates after a re start 4643 
with the identical parameters as with its predecessor.  4644 
The preconditions for this use case are  4645 
a) Devices and Master port adjustments according to 12.2.2 ; 4646 
b) Backup Level:  "Backup/Restore"  4647 
c) The replacement Device shall be re -initiated to "factory settings" in case it is not a new 4648 
Device out of the box (for " Back -to-box" see 10.7.5 ) 4649 
12.5.2  Device replacement (@ "Restore")  4650 
The stored (saved) set of back -up parameters overwrites the active parameters (e.g. factory 4651 
settings) within the replaced compatible Device of same type. This one operates after a restart 4652 
with the identical parameters as with its predecessor.  4653 
The preconditions for this use case are  4654 
a) Devices and Master port adjustments according to 12.2.2 ; 4655 
b) Backup Level:  "Restore"  4656 
12.5.3  Master replacement  4657 
12.5.3.1  General  4658 
This feature depends heavily on the implementation and integration concept of the Master de -4659 
signer and manufacturer as well as on the features of the upper level system (fieldbus).  4660 
12.5.3.2  Without fieldbus support (base level)  4661 
Principal approach for a replaced (new) Master using a Master tool:  4662 
c) Set port configurations: amongst others the Backup Level  to "Backup/Restore" or "Re store"  4663 
d) Master "reset to factory settings": clear backup parameters of all ports within the Data 4664 
Storage in case it is not a new Master out of the box  4665 
e) Active parameters of all Devices are automatically uploaded (copied) to Data Storage 4666 
(backup)  4667 
12.5.3.3  Fieldbus support (comfort level)  4668 
Any kind of fieldbus specific mechanism to back up the Master parameter set including the Data 4669 
Storage of all Devices is used. Even though these fieldbus mechanisms are similar to the IO -4670 
Link approach, they are following their certain paradigm which may conflict with the described 4671 
paradigm of the IO -Link back up mechanism (see Figure 118). 4672 
12.5.3.4  PLC system  4673 
The Device and Master parameters are stored within the system specific database of the PLC 4674 
and downloaded to the Master at system startup after replacement.  4675 
This top down concept may conflict with the active parameter setting within the Devices.   4676 
12.5.4  Project replication  4677 
Following the concept of 12.5.3.3 , the storage of complete Master parameter sets within the 4678 
parameter server of an upper level system can automatically initiate the configuration of Mas -4679 
ters and Devices besides any other upper level components and thus support the automatic 4680 
replication of machines.  4681 

===PAGE 234===
V1.1.5  – 234 – Interface and System  © IO -Link  
Following the concept of 12.5.3.4 , after supply of the Master by the PLC, the Master can supply 4682 
the Devices.  4683 
13 Integration  4684 
13.1  Generic Master model for system integration  4685 
Figure 120 shows the integration relevant excerpt of  Figure 95. Basis is the Standardized 4686 
Master Interface (SMI), which is specified in an abstract manner in 11.2 . It transforms SDCI 4687 
objects into services and objects appropriate for the upper level systems such as embedded 4688 
controllers, IT systems (JSON), fieldbuses and PLCs, engineering systems, as well as universal 4689 
Master Tools (PDCT) for Masters of different brands.  4690 
It is an objective of this SMI to achieve uniform behavior of Masters of different brands from a 4691 
user's point of view. Another objective is to provide a stringent specification for organizations 4692 
developing integration specifications into their systems without administrative overhead.  4693 
In Figure 120, the green marked items are areas of responsibility of fieldbus organizations and 4694 
their integration specifications. The blue marked items are areas of responsibility of IT 4695 
organizations  and their specifications. The red marked items are areas of responsibility of 4696 
individual automation equipment manufacturers. The white marked item ("Gateway manage -4697 
ment") represents a coordination layer for the different gateway applications. A corresponding 4698 
specification is elaborated by a joint working group [12] .  4699 
 4700 
Figure 120 – Generic Master Model  for system integration  4701 
13.2  Role of gateway applications  4702 
13.2.1  Clients  4703 
It is the role of gateway applications to provide translations of SMI services into the target 4704 
systems  (clients) . Table 105 provides an overview of specified mandatory and optional SMI 4705 
services . The designer of a gateway application determines the SMI service call technology.  4706 
Gateway applications such as shown in Figure 120 include but are not limited to:  4707 
• Pure coding tasks of the abstract SMI services, for example for embedded controllers;  4708 
• Comfortable webserver providing text and data for standard browsers using for example 4709 
XML, JSON;  4710 
• OPC-UA server used for parameterization and data exchange via IT applications; security 4711 
solutions available;  4712 
• Adapters with a fieldbus interface for programmable logic controllers (PLCs) and human 4713 
machine interfaces based on OPC -UA;  4714 
• Adapters for a User Datagram Protocol (UDP) to connect engineering tools.  4715 
13.2.2  Coordination  4716 
It is the responsibility of gateway applications to prevent from access conflicts such as  4717 
• Different clients to one Device  4718

  4721 
13.3  Security  4722 
The aspect of security is important whenever access to Master and Device data is involved. In 4723 
case of fieldbuses most of the fieldbus organizations provide dedicated guidelines on security. 4724 
In general, the IEC 62443 series is an appropriate source of protection strategies for industrial 4725 
automation applications.   4726 
13.4  Special gateway applications  4727 
13.4.1  Changing Device configuration including Data Storage  4728 
After each relevant  change of Device configuration/parameterization, the associated previously 4729 
stored data set within the Master shall be cleared or marked invalid via the variable DS_Delete.  4730 
Relevant changes via PortConfigList are:  4731 
– Change of CVID,  4732 
– Change of CDID,  4733 
– Change of Validation&Backup except changes between  "Backup  + Restore " and "Restore ", 4734 
– Change of PortMode.  4735 
 4736 
13.4.2  Parameter server and recipe control  4737 
The Master may combine the entire parameter sets of the connected Devices together with all 4738 
other relevant data for its own operation and  make this data available for upper level  4739 
applications. For example, this data may be saved within a parameter server which may be 4740 
accessed by a PLC program to change recipe parameters, thus supporting flexible 4741 
manufacturing.  4742 
NOTE   The structure of the data exchanged between the Master and the parameter server is outside the scope of 4743 
this document . 4744

13.5.1  Strategy  4746 
Figure 120 demonstrates the necessity of a tool to configure ports, parameterize the Device, 4747 
display diagnosis information, and provide identification and maintenance information. 4748 
Depending on the degree of integration into a fieldbus system, the PDCT functions can be 4749 
reduced, for example if the port configuration can be achieved via the field device description 4750 
file of the particular fieldbus (engineering).  4751

A.1  General structure and encoding  of M-sequence s 4773 
A.1.1  Overview  4774 
The general concept of M-sequence s is outlined in 7.3.3.2 . Subclauses  A.1.2  to A.1.6  provide 4775 
a detailed description of the individual elements of M-sequence s. 4776 
A.1.2  M-sequence  control  (MC) 4777 
The Master  indicates  the manner the user data  (see A.1.4 ) shall  be transmitted in an M-4778 
sequence  control octet . This indi cation includes the transmission direction  (read or write) , the 4779 
communication  channel , and the address (offset) of the data on the communication  channel. 4780 
The structure of the M-sequence  control octet  is shown  in Figure A.1. 4781 
 4782 
Figure A.1 – M-sequence  control  4783 
Bit 0 to 4: Address  4784 
These bits indicate  the address, i.e. the octet offset of the user data on the specified 4785 
communication  channel (see also Table A.1). In case of an  ISDU  channel, these bits are used 4786 
for flow control of the ISDU  data . The address, which means in this case  the position of the 4787 
user data within the ISDU , is only available  indirect ly (see  7.3.6.2 ). 4788 
Bit 5 to 6: Communication  channel  4789 
These bits indicate  the communication  channel for the access to the user data. The defined  4790 
values for the communication  channel parameter are listed in Table A.1. 4791 
Table A.1 – Values of communication  channel  4792 
Value  Definition   
0 Process  
1 Page  
2 Diagnosis  
3 ISDU  
Bit 7: R/W  4793 
This bit indicates the transmission direction of the user data on the selected communication  4794 
channel, i.e. read access (transmission of user data from Device  to Master ) or write access 4795 
(transmission of user data from Master  to Device ). The defined values for the R/W parameter 4796 
are listed in Table A.2. 4797 
Table A.2 – Values of R/W  4798 
Value  Definition   
0 Write access  
1 Read access  
A Device is not required to support each and every of the 256 values of the M -sequence control 4799 
octet. For read access to not implemented addresses or communication channels the value "0" 4800 
shall be returned. A write access to not implemented addresses or communication channels 4801 
shall be ignored.  4802

A.1.6  Calculation  of the checksum  4849 
The message  checksum provides data integrity protection for data transmission from Master  to 4850 
Device  and from Device  to Master . Each UART data octet  is protected by the UART  parity bit  4851 
(see Figure 21). Besides this individual data octet  protection , all of the UART data octet s in a 4852 
message  are XOR (exclusive or) processed octet  by octet . The check/type octet is included 4853 
with checksum bits set to "0". The resulting checksum octet is compressed from 8 to 6 bit  in 4854 
accordance with the conversion procedure in Figure A.4 and its associated formula s (see 4855 
equation s in (A.1)). The 6 bit compressed "Checksum 6" is entered into the checksum/  M-4856 
sequence type  octet ( see Figure A.2). The same procedure takes place to secure the message 4857 
from the Device  to the Master . In this case the compressed checksu m is entered into the 4858 
checksum/ status octet  (see Figure A.3). 4859 
A seed  value of 0x52 is used for the checksum calculation  across the message . It is XORed  4860 
with the first octet of the message  (MC). 4861

A.2 M-sequence  types  4865 
A.2.1  Overview  4866 
Process Data  and On-request Data  use separate cyclic and acyclic communication  channels 4867 
(see Figure 8) to ensure scheduled and deterministic delivery of Process Data  while delivery of 4868 
On-request Data  does not have consequences  on the Process Data  transmission performance . 4869 
Within SDCI , M-sequences  provide  the access to  the communication  channels via the M-4870 
sequence Control octet . The number  of different M-sequence  types meets the var ious  4871 
requirements of sensors and actuators regarding  their Process Data  width.  See Figure 39 for 4872 
an overview of  the available M-sequence  types that are specified  in A.2.2  to A.2.5 . See A.2.6  4873 
for rules on how to use the M-sequence types.  4874 
A.2.2  M-sequence  TYPE_0  4875 
M-sequence  TYPE_ 0 is mandatory for all Device s. It only transmits On-request Data . One octet 4876 
of user data is read or written per cycle. This M-sequence  is shown  in Figure A. 5. 4877

A.3 Timing constraints  4964 
A.3.1  General  4965 
The interactions of a Master  and its Device  are characterized by several time constraints that 4966 
apply to the UART frame, Master and Device message transmission times, supplemented by 4967 
response, cycle, delay, and recovery times.  4968 
A.3.2  Bit time  4969 
The bit time TBIT is the time it takes to transmit a single bit. It is the inverse value of the 4970 
transmission rate  (see equation (A.2)). 4971 
TBIT = 1/(transmission rate)  (A.2) 
Values for TBIT are specified in Table 9. 4972 

===PAGE 248===
V1.1.5  – 248 – Interface and System  © IO -Link  
A.3.3  UART frame  transmission delay of Master  (ports )  4973 
The UART frame  transmission delay t1 of a port is the duration between the end of the stop bit 4974 
of a UART frame  and the beginning of the start bit of the next UART frame . The port shall 4975 
transmit the UART frame s within a maximum delay  of one bit time  (see equation (A.3)). 4976 
0 ≤ t1 ≤ 1 TBIT (A.3) 
A.3.4  UART frame  transmission delay of  Device s 4977 
The Device ’s UART frame  transmission delay t2 is the duration between the end of the stop bit 4978 
of a UART frame  and the beginning of the start bit of the next UART frame . The Device  shall 4979 
transmit the UART frame s within a maximum delay  of 3 bit times (see equation (A.4)). 4980 
0 ≤ t2 ≤ 3 TBIT (A.4) 
A.3.5  Response time of Device s 4981 
The Device 's response time tA is the duration between the end of the stop bit of a port's last 4982 
UART frame  being received and the beginning of the start bit of the first UART frame  being 4983 
sent. The Device  shall observe a delay  of at least one bit time but no more than 10 bit times 4984 
(see equation (A.5)). 4985 
1 TBIT ≤ tA ≤ 10 TBIT (A.5) 
A.3.6  M-sequence  time  4986 
Communication between a port and and its associated Device  takes place in a fixed schedule, 4987 
called the M-sequence  time (see equation (A.6)). 4988 
t M-sequence   =  ( m+n) * 11 * TBIT  +  tA  +  ( m-1) * t1 +  ( n-1) * t2 (A.6) 
In this formula,  m is the number of UART frame s sent by the port to the Device  and n is the 4989 
number of UART frame s sent by the Device  to the port.  The formula can only be used for 4990 
estimates  as the times t1 and t2 may not be constant.  4991 
Figure A. 15 demon strates the timings of a n M-sequence  consisting of a Master  (port ) message 4992 
and a Device  message.  4993 
 4994 
Figure A. 15 – M-sequence  timing  4995

and the design and implementation of a Master  and the number of ports.   4998 
tCYC  = tM-sequence   + tidle  (A.7)

A.3.8  Idle time  5006 
The idle time tidle  results from the configured cycle time tCYC and the M-sequence  time 5007 
tM-sequence . With reference to a port, it comprises  the time between the end of the message  of 5008 
a Device  and the beginning of the next message  from the Master  (port) . 5009 
A.3.9  Recovery time  5010 
The Master shall wait for  a recovery time tinitcyc  between any two subsequent  acyclic Device 5011 
accesses  while in the STARTUP or PREOPERATE phase ( see A.2.6 ). Recovery time is defined 5012 
between the beginnings  of two subsequent Master request s. Calculations shall refer to equation 5013 
(A.7). 5014 
A.4 Error s and remedies  5015 
A.4.1  UART errors  5016 
A.4.1.1  Parity errors  5017 
The UART parity bit ( see Figure 21) and the checksum ( see A.1.6 ) are two independent 5018 
mechanisms to secure the data transfer. This means that for example two bit errors in different 5019 
octets of a messag e, which are  resulting in  the correct checksum , can also be detected. Both 5020 
mechanisms lead to the same error processing . 5021 
Remedy: The Master  shall repeat the Master  message 2 times (see 7.2.2.1 ). Devices shall 5022 
reject all data with detected errors and create no reaction.  5023 
A.4.1.2  UART framing errors  5024 
The conditions for the correct detection of a UART frame are specified  in 5.3.3.2 . Error 5025 
processing shall take place whenever perturbed signal shapes or incorrect  timings lead to  an 5026 
invalid UART stop bit . 5027 
Remedy:  See A.4.1.1 . 5028 
A.4.2  Wake -up errors  5029 
The wake -up current pulse is specified  in 5.3.3.3  and the wake -up procedures in 7.3.2.1 . 5030 
Several fa ults may oc cur during the attempts to establish communication.  5031 
Remedy: Retries are possible. See 7.3.2.1  for details . 5032 
A.4.3  Transmission  error s 5033 
A.4.3.1  Checksum errors  5034 
The checksum mechanism is specified  in A.1.6 . Any checksum error leads to an error 5035 
processing.  5036 
Remedy: See A.4.1.1 . 5037 

===PAGE 250===
V1.1.5  – 250 – Interface and System  © IO -Link  
A.4.3.2  Timeout errors  5038 
The divers e timing constraints with M-sequence s are specified  in A.3. Master  (ports)  and 5039 
Device s are checking several critical timings  such as lack of synchronism within messages . 5040 
Remedy: See A.4.1.1 . 5041 
A.4.3.3  Collisions  5042 
A collision occurs whenever the Master  and Device  are sending simultaneously due to an error. 5043 
This error is interpreted as a faulty M-sequence . 5044 
Remedy: See A.4.1.1 . 5045 
A.4.4  Protocol  error s 5046 
A protocol error occurs for example whenever the sequence of the segmented transmission of 5047 
an ISDU is wrong ( see flow control case in A.1.2 ). 5048 
Remedy: Abort of service with ErrorType  information  (see Annex  C). 5049
