A.5.4  Index and Subindex  5075 
The parameter address of the data object to be transmitted using the ISDU is specified in the 5076 
“Index” element. “Index” has a range of values from 0  to 65535  (see B.2.1  for constraints ). Index 5077 
values 0 and 1 shall be rejected  by the Device . 5078 

===PAGE 252===
V1.1.5  – 252 – Interface and System  © IO -Link  
There is no requirement  for the Device  to support all Index and Subindex values. The Device  5079 
shall send a negative response to Index or Subindex values not supported.  5080 
The data element address of a structured parameter of the data object to be transmitted using 5081 
the ISDU is specified in the “Sub index” element. “Subi ndex” has a range of values from 0  to 255, 5082 
whereby a value of "0" is used to reference the entire data object ( see Figure 6).  5083 
Table A. 15 lists  the Index  formats used in the ISDU depending o n the parameters transmitted.  5084

A.5.5  Data  5087 
The “Data” element can contain  the data  objects  specified  in Annex  B or Device  specific data 5088 
objects respectively . The data length corresponds to the entries in the “Length” element minus 5089 
the ISDU  protocol elements . 5090 
A.5.6  Check ISDU  (CHK PDU) 5091 
The “CHKPDU” element provides data integrity protection. The sender calculates the value of 5092 
"CHKPDU " by XOR processing  all of the octets  of an ISDU , including "CHKPDU" with a 5093 
preliminary value "0", which is then replaced by the result of the calculation  (see Figure A. 17).  5094 
 5095 
Figure A. 17 – Check of ISDU integrity via CHKPDU  5096 
The receiver checks whether XOR processing of all of the octets of the ISDU will lead to the 5097 
result "0" (see Figure A. 17). If the result is different from  "0", error processing shall take place. 5098 
See also A.1.6 . 5099

B.1 Direct Parameter  page 1 and 2  5219 
B.1.1  Overview  5220 
In principle, the designer of a Device  has a large amount of space for parameters and 5221 
commands as shown in Figure 6. SDCI offers the so -called Direct Parameter  pages 1 and 2 5222 
with a simplified access method ( page communication channel according to Table A.1).  5223 
The range of Direct Parameter s is structured as shown in Figure B. 1. It is split into page 1 and 5224 
page 2.  5225 
  5226 
Figure B. 1 – Classification and mapping of Direct Parameter s 5227 
Page  1 ranges from 0x00  to 0x0F. It comprises the following categories of para meters:  5228 
• Communication parameter   5229 
• Identification parameter  5230 
• Appl ication parameter   5231 
The Master  application layer (AL) provides read only access to Direct Parameter  page 1 as data 5232 
objects ( see 8.2.1 ) via Index 0 . Single octets  can be read via Index 0 and the corresponding 5233 
Subindex.  Subindex 1 indicates address 0x00 and Subindex 16 address 0x0F.  5234 
Page 2 ranges from 0x10 to 0x1F. This page comprises parameters optionally used by the 5235 
individual Device  technology. The Master  application layer (AL) provides read/write access to 5236 
Direct Parameter  page 2 in form of data objects ( see 8.2.1 ) via Index 1 . Single octets  can be 5237 
written or read via Index 1 and the corresponding Subindex.  Subindex 1 indicates address 0x10 5238 
and Subindex 16 address 0x1F.  5239 
A Device  shall always return the value "0" upon a read access to Direct Parameter  addresses, 5240 
which are not implemented (for example in case of reserved parameter addresses or not 5241 
supported optional parameters). The Device  shall ignore a  write access to not implemented 5242 
parameters.  5243 
The structure of the Direct Parameter pages 1 and 2 is specified in Table B.1. 5244

B.1.3  MasterCycleTime  and MinCycleTime  5253 
The MasterCycleTime is a Master  parameter  and sets up the actual cycle time of a particular 5254 
port.  5255 
The MinCycleTime is a Device parameter to inform the Master about the shortest cycle time 5256 
supported by this Device.  5257 
See A.3.7  for the application of the MasterCycleTime and the MinCycleTime . The structure of 5258 
these two  parameter s is shown  in Figure B. 2. 5259 
 5260 
Figure B. 2 – MinCycleTime  5261 
Bits 0 to 5: Multiplier  5262 
These bits contain a 6 -bit multiplier for the calculation of MasterCycleTime and MinCycleTime . 5263 
Permissible values for the multiplier are 0  to 63, further restrictions see Table B. 3. 5264 
Bits 6 to 7: Time Base  5265 
These bits specify  the time base for the calculation of MasterCycleTime and MinCycleTime . 5266 
In the following cases , when  5267 
• the Device provides no MinCycleTime, which is indicated by a  MinCycleTime equal zero  5268 
(binary code 0x00),  5269 
• or the MinCycleTime is shorter than the calculated M-sequence  time with the M -sequence 5270 
type used by the Device, with ( t1, t2, tidle ) equal zero and t A equal one  bit time  (see A.3.4  to 5271 
A.3.6 ) 5272 
the Master shall use the calculated worst case M -sequence timing, with the M -sequence type 5273 
used by the Device, and the maximum times for tA and t2 (see A.3.4  to A.3.6 ):  5274 
The permissible combinations for time base and multiplier are listed in Table B. 3 along with the 5275 
resulting values for MasterCycleTime or MinCycleTime . 5276

B.1.7  ProcessDataOut  5332

B.1.8  VendorID  (VID)  5335 
These octets contain a worldwide unique value per vendor.  5336 
NOTE  VendorIDs are assigned by the IO -Link community . 5337 
B.1.9  Device ID (DID)  5338 
These octets contain the currently used Device ID. A value of "0" is not permitted. It is highly 5339 
recommended to store the value of DeviceID in non -volatile memory after a compatibility switch 5340 
until a reset to the initial value through  SystemCommand s "Restore factory settings"  or " Back -5341 
to-box" . The value can be overwritten during StartUp (see 10.6.2 ). 5342 
NOTE  The communication parameters MinCycleTime , M-sequence  Capability , Process  Data  In and Process  Data   5343 
Out can be changed to a chieve compatibility to the requested DeviceID.  5344 
B.1.10  FunctionID  (FID)  5345 
This parameter w ill be defined in a later version.  5346 
B.1.11  SystemCommand  5347 
Only Device s without ISDU  support shall use the parameter SystemCommand  in the Direct 5348 
Parameter  page 1. The implementation of SystemCommand  is optional. See Table B. 9 for a 5349 
detailed description of the SystemCommand  functions.  5350 
NOTE  The SystemCommand  on the Direct Parameter page 1 does not provide a positive or negative response upon 5351 
execution of a selected function  5352 
B.1.12  Device specific Direct Parameter  page 2  5353 
The Device  specific Direct Parameter s are a set of parameters available to the Device  specific 5354 
technology. The implementation of Device  specific Direct Parameter s is optional.  It is highly 5355 
recommended for Devices (with ISDU) not to use parameters on Direct Parameter page 2.  5356 
NOTE  The complete parameter list of the Direct Parameter  page 2 is read or write accessible via index 1 (see 5357 
B.1.1 ). 5358 
B.2 Predefined Device  parameter s 5359 
B.2.1  Overview  5360 
The many different technologies and designs of sensors and actuators require individual and 5361 
easy access to complex parameters and commands beyond the capabilities of the Direct 5362 
Parameter  page 2. From a Master 's point of view, these complex parameters and commands 5363 
are c alled application data objects.  5364 
Figure B. 6 shows  the general mapping of data objects for the ISDU transmission . 5365 

===PAGE 264===
V1.1.5  – 264 – Interface and System  © IO -Link  
 5366 
Figure B. 6 – Index space for ISDU  data objects  5367 
So-called ISDU "containers" are the transfer means to exchange application data objects or 5368 
short data objects. The index of the ISDU is used to address the data objects.  5369 
Subc lause B.2 contains definitions and requirements for the implementation of technology 5370 
specific Device  applications. Implementation rules for parameters  and commands  are specified  5371 
in Table B. 7. 5372 
Table B. 7 – Implementation rules for parameters and commands  5373 
Rule number  Rule specification  
1 All parameters of an Index shall be readable and/or writeable as an entire data object via 
Subindex 0  
2 The technology specific Device application shall resolve inconsistencies of de pendent 
parameter sets during parameterization  
3 The duration of an ISDU service request is limited ( see Table 102). A master application 
can abort ISDU services after this timeout  
4 Application commands (for example teach -in, reset to factory settings, etc.) are treated 
like parameters.

 5399 
The parameter DataStorageIndex 0x0003 contains all the information to be used for the Data 5400 
Storage handling. This parameter is reserved for private exchanges between the Master and 5401 
the Device; the Master shall block any write access request from a gateway application to this 5402 
Index (see Figure 5). The parameters within this Index 0x0003 are specified as follows.  5403 
DS_Command  5404 
This octet carries the Data Storage commands for the Device . 5405 
A read operation returns un specified  values.   5406 
Note: The reaction of the DS_Command is similar to the SystemCommand, but it is assumed, that the Master 5407 
implementation will not cause any erroneous access.  5408 
State _Property  5409 
This octet indicates the current status of the Data Storage mechanism . Bit 7 shall be stored in 5410 
non-volatile memory. The Master checks this bit at start -up and performs a parameter upload if 5411 
requested.   5412 
Data_Storage_ Size  5413 
These  four  octets provide the requested memory size as number of  octets  for storing all the 5414 
information required for the replacement of a Device  including the structural information (Index, 5415 
Subindex) . Data type is U IntegerT 32 (32 bit).  The maximum size is 2  048 octets. See Table G.1 5416 
for the elements to be taken into account in the size calculation . 5417 
Parameter_ Checksum  5418 
This checksum is used to detect changes in the parameter set without reading all parameters. 5419 
The value of the checksum is calculated according to the procedure in 10.4.8 . The Device  shall 5420 
change the checksum whenever a parameter out of the parameter set has been altered. 5421 
Different parameter sets shall hold different checksums.  It is recommended that the Device 5422 
stores this parameter locally in non -volatile memory.  5423 
Index_ List  5424

(see Table 102). 5426

Large sets of parameters  can be handled via concatenated Index_ List s. The last two octets of 5429 
the Index_ List shall carry the Termination Marker. A value "0" indi cates the end of the Index 5430 
List . In case of concatenation the Termination Marker is set to the next Index containing an 5431 
Index List. The structure of the following Index List is the same as specified  in Table B. 11. Thus, 5432 
the concatenation of lists ends if a Termination Marker with the value "0" is found.  5433 
B.2.4  Device Access Locks  5434 
The parameter DeviceAccessLocks allows control of the Device  behaviour. Standardized 5435 
Device  functions can independently be configured via defined flags in this parameter. The 5436 
DeviceAccessLocks configuration can be changed by overwriting the parameter. The actual 5437 
configuration setting is available per read access to this parameter. The data type is RecordT  5438 
of BooleanT . Access is only permitted via Subindex  0.  5439 
This parameter is optional.  If implemented it shall be non -volatile.  5440 
The following Device  access lock  categories are specified . 5441 
• Parameter write access ( obsolete ) 5442 
• Data Storage  (obsolete ) 5443 
• Local paramet erization ( optional ) 5444 
• Local user interface operation ( optional ) 5445

Parameter (write) access:  5450 
If this bit is set, w rite access to all Device  parameters over the SDCI communication interface 5451 
is inhibited for all read/write parameters of the Device  except the parameter Device Access 5452 
Locks . Read access is not affected. The Device  shall respond with the negative service 5453 
response  – access denied – to a write access, if the parameter access is locked.  5454 
The parameter (write) access lock mechanism shall not block downloads of the Data Storage 5455 
mechanism (between DS_DownloadStart and DS_DownloadEnd or DS_Break).  5456 
Data Storage : 5457 
If this bit is set  in the Device , the Data Storage  mechanism is disabled (see 10.4.2  and 11.4.4 ). 5458 
In this case, t he Device  shall respond to a write access (within its Data Storage Index) with a 5459 
negative service response – access denied – (see B.2.3 ). Read access to its DataStorageIndex 5460 
is not affected.  5461 
This setting is also indicated in the State Property within Data Storage Index . 5462 
Local parameterization : 5463 
If this bit is set, the parameterization  via local control elements on the Device  is inhibited  (write 5464 
protection). Read only is possible (see 10.6.7 ). 5465 
Local user interface : 5466 
If this bit is set, operation of the human machine  interface on the Device  is disabled  (see 10.6.8 ). 5467

B.2.21  DeviceStatus  5539 
B.2.21.1  Overview  5540 
The parameter DeviceStatus  shall provide information about the Device  condition  (diagnosis)  5541 
by the Device 's technology . The data type is UIntegerT with a bitLength of 8. The parameter is 5542 
a read -only data object. This parameter is optional.  5543 
The following Device  conditions in Table B. 13 are specified . They shall be generated  by the 5544 
Device  application s, the relation to the DetailedDeviceStatus is defined in 10.10.1 . The 5545 
parameter DeviceStatus  can be read by any PLC program  or tools such as Asset Management 5546 
(see Clause  11). 5547 
Table B. 13 lists  the different DeviceStatus  info rmation . The criteria for these indications are 5548 
specified  in subclauses  B.2.21.3  through B.2.21.6 . The priority column defines which status 5549 
value is signalled in case of multiple active events, the lowest priority value dominates higher 5550 
priority values.  5551 
Table B. 13 – DeviceStatus  parameter  5552 
Value  Priority   Definition  
0 5 Device is operating properly (see B.2.21.2 )  
1 3 Maintenance -Required (see B.2.21.3 ) 
2 4 Out-of-Specification (see B.2.21.4 ) 
3 2 Functional -Check (see B.2.21.5 ) 
4 1 Failure (see B.2.21.6 ) 
5 – 255  - Reserved  
 5553 
B.2.21.2  Device is operating properly  5554 
The Device is working without any impairment and no Event is pending, see B.2.22 . 5555 
B.2.21.3  Maintenance -required  5556 
Although the Process Data  are valid, internal diagnostics  indicate that the Device is close to 5557 
lose its ability of correct functioning.  5558 
EXAMPLES  Optical lenses getting dusty, build -up of deposits, lubricant level low.  5559 
B.2.21.4  Out-of-Specification  5560 
Although the Process Data  are valid, internal diagnostics indicate that the Device is operating  5561 
outside its specified measuring range or environmental conditions . 5562 
EXAMPLES  Power supply, auxiliary energy, temperature, pneumatic pressure, magnetic interference, vibrations, 5563 
acceleration, interfering light, bubble formation in liquids.  5564 
B.2.21.5  Functional -Check  5565 
User intended manipulations on the Device are ongoing and the Device may not be able to 5566 
provide valid  Process Data.  5567 
EXAMPLES  Calibrations, position adjustments, and simulation.  5568 
B.2.21.6  Failure  5569 
The Device is unable to perform its intended function.  The Process Data shall  be marked as 5570 
invalid if no part of the process data content can be provided . In the case of partially invalid 5571 
process data, the process data may be marked as invalid at the discretion of the device 5572 
manufacturer. The method of indicating partially invalid process data content is profile or vendor 5573 
specific .  5574 
B.2.22  DetailedDevice Status  5575 
The parameter DetailedDeviceStatus shall provide information about currently pending Events 5576 
in the Device. Events of TYPE "Error" or "Warning" and MODE "Event appears" ( see A.6.4 ) 5577 

===PAGE 273===
Interface and System  © IO -Link  – 273 – V1.1.5  
shall be entered into the list of DetailedDeviceStatus with EventQualifier and Event Code. Upon 5578 
occurrence of an Event with MODE "Event disappears", the corresponding entry in 5579 
DetailedDeviceStatus shall be set to EventQualifier "0x00" and EventCode "0x0000". This way 5580 
this parameter always provides the current diagnosis status of the Device. The parameter is a 5581 
read -only data object. The data type is ArrayT with a maximum number of 64 array elements 5582 
(Event entries). The number of array elements of this parameter is Device specific. Upon power -5583 
off or reset of the Device the contents of all array elements are set to initial settings  – 5584 
EventQualifier "0x00", EventCode "0x0000". This parameter is optional.  5585

 5636 
Error Types (ISDU errors)  5637 
C.1 General  5638 
An Error Type is used within negative service confirmations of ISDU s (see A.5.2  and Table A. 13) 5639 
or negative acknowledgements of SMI services (see E.18 ). It indicates the cause of a negative  5640 
confirmation of a Read or Write service. The origin  of the error may be located in the M aster 5641 
(local) or in the Device (remote).  5642 
The Error Type consists of two octets , the main e rror  cause and more  specific information : 5643 
• Error Code (high order octet)  5644 
• Additional Code (low order octet)  5645 
The Error Type  represents  information about the incident , the origin and the instance. The 5646 
permissible Error Types  and the criteria for their deployment are listed in  C.2, C.3, and C.4. All 5647 
other Error Type  values are reserved and shall not be used . 5648 
C.2 Application related ErrorTypes  5649

C.2.2  Device application error – no details  5654 
This ErrorType shall be used if the requested service has been refused by the Device  5655 
application and no detailed information of the incident is available.  5656 
C.2.3  Index not available  5657 
This ErrorType shall be used whenever a read or write access occurs to a no n-existing Index  5658 
with or without Subindex access . 5659 
C.2.4  Subindex not available  5660 
This ErrorType shall be used whenever a read or write access occurs to a no n-existing Subindex  5661 
of an existing Index . 5662 
C.2.5  Service temporarily not available  5663 
This ErrorType shall be used if a parameter is not accessible for a read or write service due to 5664 
the current state of the Device  application.  5665 
C.2.6  Service temporarily not available – local control  5666 
This ErrorType shall be used if a parameter is not accessible for a read or write service due to 5667 
an ongoing local operation at the Device  (for example operation or parameterization via an on -5668 
board Device control panel).  5669 
C.2.7  Service temporarily not available – device control  5670 
This ErrorType shall be used if a read or write service is not accessible due to a remote triggered 5671 
state of the device application (for example parameterization  during a remote triggered teach -5672 
in operation or calibration).  5673 
C.2.8  Access denied  5674 
This ErrorType shall be used if a Write service tries to access a read -only parameter  or if a 5675 
Read service tries to access a write -only parameter.  5676 
C.2.9  Parameter value out of range  5677 
This ErrorType shall be used for a write service to a parameter outside  its permitted range  of 5678 
values.  Example: enumerations (list of single values), combination of value ranges and 5679 
enumeration.  5680 
C.2.10  Parameter value above limit  5681 
This ErrorType shall be used for a write service to a parameter above its specified value range.  5682 
C.2.11  Parameter value below limit  5683 
This ErrorType shall be used for a write service to a parameter below its specified value range.  5684 

===PAGE 277===
Interface and System  © IO -Link  – 277 – V1.1.5  
C.2.12  Parameter length overrun  5685 
This ErrorType shall be used when the content of  a write service to a parameter is greater than 5686 
the parameter  specified length. This ErrorType shall also be used, if a data object is too large 5687 
to be processed by the Device  application (for example ISDU  buffer restriction).  5688 
C.2.13  Parameter length underrun  5689 
This ErrorType shall be used when the content of a write service to a parameter is less than 5690 
the parameter specified length  (for example write access of an Unsigned16 value to an 5691 
Unsigned32 parameter).  5692 
C.2.14  Function not available  5693 
This ErrorType shall be used for a write service with a command value not supported by the 5694 
Device  application (for example a SystemCommand  with a value not implemented).  5695 
C.2.15  Function temporarily unavailable  5696 
This ErrorType shall be used for a write service with a command value calling a Device  function 5697 
not available due to the current state of the Device  application (for example a 5698 
SystemCommand ). 5699 
C.2.16  Invalid parameter set 5700 
This ErrorType shall be used if values sent via single parameter transfer are not consistent with 5701 
other actual parameter settings (for example overlapping set points for a binary data setting; 5702 
see 10.3.4 ). 5703 
C.2.17  Inconsistent parameter set 5704 
This ErrorType shall be used at the termination of a Block Parameter  transfer with 5705 
ParamDownloadEnd or ParamDownloadStore  or after a DS_DownloadEnd Command,  if the 5706 
plausibility check shows inconsi stencies (see 10.3.5 , B.2.2 , and 10.4.1 ). 5707 
C.2.18  Application not ready  5708 
This ErrorType shall be used if a read or write service is refused due to a temporarily 5709 
unavailable application (for example peripheral controllers during startup).  5710 
C.2.19  Vendor specific  5711 
This ErrorType will be propagated directly to upper level  processing elements as an error (no 5712 
warning) by the Master . 5713 
  5714 

===PAGE 278===
V1.1.5  – 278 – Interface and System  © IO -Link

C.4.2  ArgBlock unknown  5751 
This ErrorType shall be used if the requested ArgBlockID is unknown to the SMI.  5752 
C.4.3  Incorrect ArgBlock content type  5753 
This ErrorType shall be used if the SMI service detects errors in the structure of the provided 5754 
ArgBlock . 5755 
C.4.4  Device not communicating  5756 
This ErrorType shall be used if the Port is not communicating with the Device.  5757 
C.4.5  Service unknown  5758 
This ErrorType shall be used if a requested SMI service is not supported by the Master.  5759 
C.4.6  Process Data not accessible  5760 
This ErrorType shall be used if the requested Process Data cannot be accessed  in current state 5761 
of communication.  5762 
C.4.7  Insufficient memory  5763 
This ErrorType shall be used if the requested SMI service requires more memory space.  5764 
C.4.8  Incorrect Port number  5765 
This ErrorType shall be used if the requested Port number is invalid.  5766 

===PAGE 280===
V1.1.5  – 280 – Interface and System  © IO -Link  
C.4.9  Incorrect ArgBlock content  5767 
This ErrorType shall be used if the actual ArgBlock content is not consistent or contains invalid 5768 
data.  5769 
C.4.10  Incorrect ArgBlock length  5770 
This ErrorType shall be used if the actual ArgBlock length does not correspond to the 5771 
ArgBlockID.  5772 
C.4.11  Master busy  5773 
This ErrorType shall be used if the SMI service is blocked  due to other running processes . 5774 
C.4.12  Inconsistent DS data  5775 
This ErrorType shall be used if Data Storage is not supported or Data Storage is not activated 5776 
on this Port or Data Storage content is not consistent with Port configuration, for example 5777 
VendorID does not match.  5778 
C.4.13  Device/ Master error  5779 
These ErrorTypes from Device or Master Port are propagated if the requested SMI service has 5780 
been denied by the Device.   5781

Event Codes (diagnosis information)  5785 
D.1 General  5786 
The concept of Event s is described  in 7.3.8.1  and the general structure and encoding of Event s 5787 
is specified  in Clause A.6. Whenever the Status Code indicates an Event  in case of a Device  or 5788 
a Master  incident, t he associated EventCode shall be provided as diagnosis information. As 5789 
speci fied in  A.6, the Event  entry contains an EventCode in addition to the Event Qualifier. The 5790 
EventCode identifies an  actual incident . Permissible values  for EventCode are listed in  Table 5791 
D.1; all other EventCode values are reserved and shall not be used . 5792

D.3 EventCodes for Ports  5799 
Table D. 2 lists the specified EventCode identifiers and their definitions  for Ports . The 5800 
EventCodes are created by the Master  (Source = "Master /Port", see Table A. 18, and 5801 
"application" (APP) or "communication system" (SYS) as INSTANCE, see Table Table A. 17). 5802 
EventCode identifiers 0xFF21 to 0xFFFF are internal system infor mation and shall not be visible 5803 
to users.   5804 
The following rules apply:   5805 
– Port Events referring to SDCI communication are mandatory (exceptions 0xFF26/0xFF27) 5806 
and are specified in detail (Event INSTANCE = SYS). The other Port Events (Event 5807 
INSTANCE = APP) are optional.  5808 
– Each appearing Port Event of Type "Error" requires a disappearing Port Event whenever the 5809 
cause of the Error has been fixed.  5810 
– Occurring PortStatusInfo "PORT_DIAG" leads to an appearing EventCode 0x180x or 0x600x 5811 
depending on "SYS" Error (see Table 126). 5812 
– Leaving PortStatusInfo "PORT_DIAG" to others leads to disappearing EventCodes for each 5813 
pending Error (0x180x).  5814 
– Every appearing/disappearing Event leads to an update of the DiagEntry section in the 5815 
PortStatusList (see Table E. 4). 5816

Annex  E 5822

 5824 
Coding of ArgBlocks  5825 
E.1 General  5826 
The purpose of ArgBlocks is explained in 11.2.2 . Each ArgBlock is uniquely defined by its 5827 
ArgBlock identifi er (ArgBlockID) and its ArgBlock length (ArgBlockLength). Extension of 5828 
ArgBlocks by just using a larger  ArgBlock length is not permitted. Manufacturer specific 5829 
ArgBlocks are possible by using the service groups B to E (see  Figure E. 1).  5830 
Transmission of ArgBlocks is following the convention in Figure E. 1 as octet stream beginning 5831 
with octet offset 0.  5832 
The four -nibble  structure of the ArgBlockID is shown in  Figure E. 1 5833 
The ArgBlockID "0x0000" is reserved. The fourth nibble (N4) is assigned to SMI service groups. 5834 
The third nibble  (N3)  is assigned to domains and to SMI management.  Nibble 1 (N1) and nibble 5835 
2 (N2) define ArgBlocks within the particular domain.   5836 
 5837 
Figure E. 1 – Assignment of ArgBlock identifiers  5838 
Table E.1 shows all defined ArgBlock types and their IDs  including those for system extensions 5839 
in order to avoid  ambiguities . ArgBlockIDs are assigned by the IO -Link Community.  5840 
Table E.1 – ArgBlock types and their ArgBlockIDs  5841

F.1 General  5975 
This annex specifies  basic  and composite  data types . Examples demonstrate the structure s and 5976 
the transmission aspects of data types for singular  use or in a packed manner.  5977 
NOTE  More examples are available in [6] . 5978 
F.2 Basic  data types  5979 
F.2.1  General  5980 
The coding of basic  data types is shown only for singular use , which is characterized by  5981 
• Process Data  consisting of one basic  data type  5982 
• Parameter consisting of one basic  data type  5983 
• Subindex (>0) access on individual data items  of parameters of composite  data types  5984 
(arrays, records)   5985 
F.2.2  BooleanT  5986 
A Boolean T is representing a data type that can have only two different values i.e. TRUE and 5987 
FALSE.  The data type is specified  in Table F.1. For singular use the  coding is shown in Table 5988 
F.2. A sender shall always use 0xFF for 'T RUE' or 0x00 for 'FALSE'. Since some upperlevel 5989 
software tools are not used to this restricted use of Booleans, a receiver can interpret the range 5990 
from 0x01 through 0xFE for 'TRUE' or reject with an error message.  The packed form is 5991 
demonstrated in Table F.22 and Figure F.9. 5992 
Table F.1 – BooleanT  5993 
Data type name  Value range  Resolution  Length  
Boolean T TRUE / FALSE  - 1 bit or 1  octet  
 5994 
Table F.2 – BooleanT coding  5995 
Bit  7 6 5 4 3 2 1 0 Values  
TRUE  1 1 1 1 1 1 1 1 0xFF  
FALSE  0 0 0 0 0 0 0 0 0x00

F.2.5  Float32T  6030 
A Float32 T is representing a number specified  by IEEE  Std 754-1985  as single precision (32 6031 
bit). Table F.9 gives  the definition and Table F.10 the coding. SN represents the sign with "0" 6032 
for all positive numbers and zero, and "1" for all negative numbers.  6033

Table F.10 – Coding of Float32T  6036 
Bits  7 6 5 4 3 2 1 0 
Octet 1  SN Exponent (E)  
 20 27 26 25 24 23 22 21 
Octet 2  (E)  Fraction (F)  
 20 2-1 2-2 2-3 2-4 2-5 2-6 2-7

  6037 
In order to realize negative exponent values a special exponent encoding mechanism is set in 6038 
place as follows:  6039 
The Float32T exponent (E) is encoded using an offset binary representation, with the zero offset 6040 
being 127; also known as exponent bias in IEEE  Std 754-1985 . 6041 
Emin = 0x01 - 0x7F  = -126 6042 
Emax = 0xFE - 0x7F = 127  6043 
Exponent bias = 0x7F = 127  6044 
Thus, as defined by the offset binary representation, in order to get the true exponent the offset 6045 
of 127 shall  be subtr acted from the stored exponent.  6046 
F.2.6  StringT  6047 
A StringT is representing an ordered sequence of symbols  (characters)  with a variable or fixed 6048 
length of octets (maximum of 232 octets) coded in US-ASCII (7 bit) or UTF-8. UTF -8 uses one 6049 
octet for all ASCII characters and up to 4 octets for  other characters . 0x00 is not permi tted as 6050 
a character.  Table F.11 gives  the definition.  6051 
Table F.11 – StringT  6052 
Data type name  Encoding  Standards  Length  a 
StringT  US-ASCII  see ISO/IEC  646 Any length of character string with a maximum of 
232 octets  
UTF-8 b see ISO/IEC  10646   
NOTE  a  Length can  be obtained from a Device's IODD via the attribute 'fixedLength'.  
NOTE b   In order to ensure proper handling of client applications it is recommended not to use US - 
               ASCII or UTF -8 codes from 0x00 to 0x1F and 0xFF . 
 6053 
An instance of StringT can be shorter than defined by the IODD attribute 'fixedLength'. 0x00 6054 
shall be used for the padding of unused octets.  6055 
A condensed form can be used for optimization, where the character string is transmitted in its 6056 
actual length and the padding octets are omitted. The receiver can deduce the original length 6057 
from the length of the ISDU or by searching the first NULL (0x00) character (see A.5.2  and 6058 
A.5.3 ). This condensed form can be used in case of singular access (see Figure F.4). 6059 

===PAGE 302===
V1.1.5  – 302 – Interface and System  © IO -Link

F.2.7  OctetStringT  6062 
An OctetStringT is representing an ordered sequence of octets with a fixed length (maximum 6063 
of 232 octets). Table F.12 gives  the definition  and Figure F.5 a coding example  for a fixed length 6064 
of 7 . 6065

F.2.8  TimeT  6070 
A TimeT is based on the RFC  1305 standard and composed of two unsigned values that express 6071 
the network time related to a particular date. Its semantic has changed from RFC  1305 6072 
according to  Figure F.6. Table F.13 gives  the definition and Table F.14 the coding of TimeT.  6073 
The first element is a  32-bit unsigned integer  data type that provides the network time in 6074 
seconds since 1900 -01-01 0.00,00(UTC) or since 2036 -02-07 6.28,16(UTC) for time values less 6075 
than 0x9DFF4400, which represents the 1984 -01-01 0:00,00(UTC). The second element is a 6076 
32-bit unsigned integer  data type that provides the fractional portion of seconds in 1/2 32 s. 6077 
Rollovers after 136 years are not automatically detectable and shall  be maintained by the 6078 
application.   6079

F.3 Composite  data types  6097 
F.3.1  General  6098 
Composite  data types are combinations of basic  data types  only . A composite data type consists 6099 
of several basic data types packed within a sequence of octets. Unused bit space shall be 6100 
padded with "0".  6101 
F.3.2  Array T 6102 
An Array T addressed by an  Index  is a data structure with data items of the same data type. The 6103 
individual data items are addressable by the Subindex . Subindex  0 addresses the whole array 6104 
within the Index  space.  The s tructuring rules for arrays are given  in Table F.17. 6105 
Table F.17 – Structuring rules for ArrayT  6106 
Rule number  Rule specification  
1 The Subindex data items are packed in a row without gaps describing an octet sequence  
2 The highest Subindex data item n starts right aligned  within the octet sequence  
3 UIntegerT and IntegerT with a length of ≥ 58 bit and < 64 bit are not permitted  
 6107 
Table F.18 and Figure F.7 give an example for the access of an array. Its content is a set of 6108 
parameters  of the same basic data type.  6109

F.3.3  Record T 6114 
A record addressed by an Index  is a data structure with data items of different data types. The 6115 
Subindex  allows addressing  individual data items within the record on certain bit positions . 6116 
NOTE  Bit positions within a RecordT  may be obtained from the IODD of the particular Device . 6117 
The structuring rules for records are  given  in Table F.19. 6118 
Table F.19 – Structuring rules for RecordT  6119 
Rule number  Rule specification  
1 The Subindices within the IODD shall be listed in ascending order from 1 to n describing an 
octet sequence. Gaps within the list of Subindices are allowed  
2 Bit offsets shall always be indicated within this octet sequence (may show no strict order in 
the IODD)  
3 The bit offset starts with the last octet within the sequence; this octet starts with offset 0 for 
the least significant bit and offset 7 for the most significant bit  
4 The following data types shall always be aligned on octet boundaries: Float32T, StringT, 
OctetStringT, TimeT, and TimeSpanT  
5 UIntegerT and IntegerT with a length of ≥ 58 bit shall always be aligned on one side of an 
octet boundary  
6 It is highly recommended for UIntegerT and IntegerT with a length of ≥ 8 bit to align always 
on one side of an octet boundary  
7 It is highly recommended for UIntegerT and IntegerT with a length of < 8 bit not to cross 
octet boundaries  
8 A bit position shall not be used by more than one record item  
 6120

named  "Status ", "Text",  and " Value ". 6122

Annex  H 6155 
(normative)  6156 
 6157 
Master and Device  conformity  6158 
H.1 Electromagnetic compatibility  requirements (EMC)  6159 
H.1.1  General  6160 
The EMC requirements of this specification are only relevant for the SDCI interface part of a 6161 
particular Master or Device. The technology functions of a Device and its relevant EMC 6162 
requirements are not in the scope of this specification. For this purpose,  the Device specific 6163 
product standards shall apply.  For Master  usually the EMC requirements for peripherals are 6164 
specified  in IEC 61131 -2 or IEC 61000 -6-2. 6165 
To ensure proper operating conditions of the SDCI  interface, the test configurations specified  6166 
in section H.1.6  (Master) or H.1.7  (Device ) shall be maintained during all the EMC tests. The 6167 
tests required in the product standard of equipment  under test ( EUT) can alternatively be 6168 
performed in SIO mode . 6169 
H.1.2  Operating conditions  6170 
It is highly recommended to evaluate the SDCI during the startup phase with the cycle times 6171 
given in Table H.1. In most cases, this leads to the minimal time requirements for  the 6172 
perform ance of  these tests. Alternatively , the SDCI  may be evaluated during normal operation 6173 
of the Device , provided  that the required number of M-sequence s specified in Table H.1 took 6174 
place  during each test.  6175 
In case a test requires longer M -sequences than an M -sequence group specified in Table H.1, 6176 
the error criteria shall be applied to every M-sequence group.  6177 
In case of Class B devices it is recommended to perform the EMC test under maximum ripple 6178 
and load switching on Power 2.  6179 
H.1.3  Performance criteria  6180 
a) Performance criterion A  6181 
The SDCI operating at an average cycle time as specified  in Table H.1 shall not show more 6182 
than six detected M-sequence  errors  with in the number of M-sequence s given in Table H.1. 6183 
Multiple kinds of errors within one M -sequence shall be counted as one error.  No interruption 6184 
of communication is permitted . 6185 
Table H.1 – EMC test conditions for SDCI  6186

b) Performance Criterion B  6188 
The error rate of criterion A shall also be satisfied after but not during the test. No change of 6189 
actual operating state (e.g. permanent loss of communication) or stored data is allowed.  6190

The following requirements also apply as specified in Table H.2. 6195 
a) As this phenomenon influences the entire device under test, an existing device specific 6196 
product standard shall take precedence over the test levels specified here.  6197 
b) The test shall be performed with a step size of 1 % and a dwell of 1 s. If a single M -sequence 6198 
error occurs at a certain frequency, that frequency shall be tested until the number of M -6199 
sequences according to Table H.1 has been transmitted or until 6 M -sequence errors 6200 
occurred.  6201 
c) Depending on the transmission rate the test time varies. The test time shall be at least one 6202 
minute (with the transmitted M -sequences and the permitted errors increased accordingly).  6203 
d) This phenomenon is expected to influence most probably the EUTs internal analog signal 6204 
processing and only with a very small probability the functionality of the SDCI 6205 
communication. Therefore,  an existing device specific product standard shall take 6206 
precedence over the test levels specified here.  6207 
e) Measurement shall be performed at least for three orthogonal orientations of the Device 6208 
with respect to the direction of the electromagnetic wave propagation.  6209

The definition of emission limits is not in the scope of this specification. The requirements of 6212 
the Device  specific product family or generic standards apply, usually for general industrial 6213 
environments the IEC 61000 -6-4.  6214

All emission tests shall be performed at the fastest possible communication rate with the fastest 6215 
cycle time.  6216 
H.1.6  Test configurations for Master  6217 
H.1.6.1  General rules  6218 
The following  rules apply for the test of Master s:  6219 
• In the following test setup diagrams only the SDCI and the power supply cables are shown. 6220 
All other cables shall be treated as required by the relevant product standard.  6221 
• Grounding of power supply,  Master , and Device s shall be according to the relevant product 6222 
standard or manual.  6223 
• Where not otherwise stated, the SDCI  cable shall have an overall length of 20 m. Excess 6224 
length laid as an inductive coil with a diameter of 0,3 m, where applicable mounted 0,1 m 6225 
above reference ground.  6226 
• Where applicable, the auxiliary Device s shall be placed 10 cm above RefGND.  6227 
• A typical test configuration consists of the Master  and two Device s, except for the RF 6228 
common mode test, where only one Device  shall be used.  6229 
• Each port shall fulfill the EMC requirements.  6230

H.2 Test strategies  for conformity  6278 
H.2.1  Test of a Device  6279 
The Master  AUX 1 ( see Figure H.5 to Figure H.8) shall continuo usly send a n M-sequence  6280 
TYPE_ 0 (read Direct P arameter page 2) message  at the cycle time specified  in Table H.1 and 6281 
count the missing and the erroneous Device  response s. Both numbers shall be added  and 6282 
indicated . 6283 
NOTE   Detailed instructions for the Device tests are specified in [9] . 6284

Process Data shall be generated by an 8 bit random or pseudo random generator. The Master 6287 
shall copy the input Process Data of any received Device message to the output Process Data 6288

I.1 Residual error probability of the SDCI data integrity mechanism  6304 
Figure I.1 shows the residual error probability ( REP) of the SDCI data integrity mechanism 6305 
consisting of the checksum data integrity procedure ("XOR6 ") as specified  in A.1.6  and the 6306 
UART parity . The diagram refers to IEC 60870 -5-1 with its data integrity class  I2 for a minimum 6307 
Hamming distance of 4 (red dotted line) . 6308 
 6309 
Figure I.1 – Residual error prob ability for the SDCI data integrity mechanism  6310 
The blue line shows the residual error curve for a data length of 2 octets. The black  curve shows 6311 
the residual error curve for a data length of 3 octets. The purple curve shows the residual error 6312 
curve for a data length of 4 octets.  6313 
I.2 Derivation of EMC test conditions  6314 
The p erformance criterion A in H.1.3  is derived from  requirements specified  in IEC 61158 -2 in 6315 
respect to interference susceptibility and error rates  (citation ; " frames"  translates into 6316 
"messages" within this standard ): 6317 
• Only 1 undetected errone ous frame  in 20 years at 1  600 frames /s 6318 
• The ratio of undetected to detec ted frames  shall not exceed 10 -6 6319 
• EMC tests shall not show more than 6 erroneous frames  within 100  000 frames  6320 
With SDCI, t he first requirement transforms  into the Equation (I.1). This equation allows 6321 
determining a value of BEP. The equation can be resolved in a numerical  way.  6322

Annex  K 6352 
(informative)  6353 
 6354 
Recommended methods for detecting parameter changes  6355 
K.1 CRC signature  6356 
Cyclic Redundancy Checking belongs to the HASH function family. A CRC signature across all 6357 
changeable parameters can be calculated by the Device with the help of a so -called proper 6358 
generator polynomial. The calculation results in a different signature whenever the parameter 6359 
set has been changed. It should be noted that the signature secures also the octet order within 6360 
the parameter set. Any change in the order when calculating the signature will lead to a different 6361 
value. The quality of securing (undetected changes) depends heavily on both the CRC 6362 
generator polynomial and the length (number of octets) of the parameter set. The seed value 6363 
should be > 0. One calculation method uses directly the formula, another one uses octet shifting 6364 
and lookup tables. The first one requests less program memory and is a bit slower, the other 6365 
one requires memory for a lookup table (1 x 210 octets for a 32-bit signature) and is fast.  The 6366 
parameter data set comparison is performed in state "Checksum_9" of the Data Storage (DS) 6367 
state machine in Figure 104. Table K.1 lists several possible generator polynomials and their 6368 
detection level.  6369 
Table K.1 – Proper CRC generator polynomials  6370 
Generator polynomial  Signature  Data length  Undetected changes  
0x9B  8 bits  1 octet  < 2-8 (not recommended)  
0x4EAB  16 bits  1 < octets < 3  < 2-16 (not recommended)  
0x5D6DCB  24 bits  1 < octets < 4  < 2-24 (not recommended)  
0xF4ACFB13  32 bits  1 < octets < 2 32 < 2-32 (recommended)  
  6371 
K.2 Revision counter  6372 
A 32-bit revision counter can be implemented, counting any change of the parameter set. The 6373 
Device shall use a random initial value for the Revision Counter. The counter itself shall not be 6374 
stored via Index List of the Device. After the download the actual counter value is read back 6375 
from the Device to avoid multiple writing initiated by the download sequence. The parameter 6376 
data set comparison is performed in state "Checksum_9" of the Data Storage (DS) state 6377 
machine in Figure 104. 6378
